// v3.9d (4 Nov 2026): no OK item that only goes on.  Roger, 4 Nov 2026:
//   use "Run" or "Solve" or "Exit" but NOT "OK" the way HP uses it
//   (on the Prime's own screens OK closes the screen).  The menu
//   "Nothing to add to the pool" offered OK to carry on; its item now
//   reads "Go on".  Its number (1) and every path are unchanged, and
//   the three version strings on screen name v3.9d.
//   And the monkey test's finding (1,000 runs on v3.9c: 977 OK, 23 a
//   list typed into a number field, which went into the arithmetic):
//   every field of the four forms takes a real number only,
//   {name,[0]} (Lessons Learned s4).
//
// v3.9c (20 Oct 2026): symbols instead of spelled-out names, on screen
//   only (Roger, 20 Oct 2026: "use the symbols we developed").  In the
//   text the student sees, the help, Bang & Ramm and Lamendin pages' "sqrt(" and "^2" now
//   use the house glyphs: chi-square (CHAR(967) CHAR(178), device-verified),
//   ^2 (CHAR(178)), sqrt (CHAR(8730)), and the x-bar and p-hat glyphs,
//   CHAR(57344) and CHAR(57345), HP's own private-use characters.  Text built for the CAS is
//   untouched (it must stay ASCII), and so are Home variable names
//   and comments.  No number, layout position or logic changed.
//   Second pass, same day, after all 20 programs were checked: "+/-",
//   ">=", "<=", "x1.25", "mu", "sigma", "pi", "->", "*" and "^2"
//   now use +/- (CHAR(177)), >= (CHAR(8805)), <= (CHAR(8804)), x (CHAR(215)),
//   mu (CHAR(956)), ^2 (CHAR(178)), pi (CHAR(960), new on screen), ->
//   (CHAR(8594)), sigma (CHAR(963)), sqrt (CHAR(8730)).  The "+/-" that names the
//   + and - keys is kept.
//   In this program the glyphs are built with CHAR(), as its other
//   display glyphs are, so the source stays pure ASCII.
//
//
// WHAT CHANGED IN v3.9b  (19 Oct 2026; line numbers are v3.9a's)
//   One wording fix, found by the error genealogy of 19 Oct 2026
//   ("How the Errors Travelled"; researcher A's notes, A1 and A3).
//   v3.9a's Gustafson page said his +/- 3.63 "is a mean deviation"
//   and that his line was "fitted to estimated, not real, ages".
//   Both are Maples & Rice's 1979 readings.  Lucy & Pollard (1995,
//   pp 223-224) could not reproduce 3.63 as an average deviation
//   (5.34 or 8.63 yr) nor recalculate the line; Aykroyd (1995, p 925)
//   derived 3.63 exactly: the prediction error of a MEAN, computed
//   with the sample SD of the ages where the regression's SD
//   belonged.  So 3.63 is not the error of one estimate, and "fitted
//   to estimated ages" is Maples & Rice's inference, not a fact.
//   - WHY GUSTAFSON 1950 IS EXCLUDED (5310-5318): Maples & Rice keep
//     what is sure (the circularity, pp 168-169; the refitted SE
//     +/- 7.03, p 170); a new paragraph gives Aykroyd's derivation.
//     18 lines, no blank: page 1 (13 lines) still ends after
//     Lamendin's paragraph and page 2 opens with Lucy's.
//   - This header's WHY GUSTAFSON (822-828) says the same, and
//     REFERENCES add Lucy & Pollard 1995 and Aykroyd 1995.
//   - Version strings: the banner, the opening page, About's first
//     title, help topic 10 and the checklist name.
//   - NOT changed: every routine but FDAES() and fdHelp (whose only
//     change is the version string); every number, table, equation,
//     menu and page except the lines above.
//

//   recomputation: only the honest minimum is done for them.  Every

//   independent readings, every cell agreeing).
//   - THE VERDICT BAND (D1).  Symmetric, stated cut-points, printed in
//     the band: P(age >= 18) >= 95 % green "probably >= 18", <= 5 %
//     red "probably < 18", otherwise orange "uncertain"; both ends
//     inclusive, tested on P as printed (0.1 %), so the colour and the

//     offered to the pool; the odontogram reads "Willems 2001: at the

//     printed "Dental age 16.03 yr" and "likely < 18  P(age >= 18) =

//     1049-1073, fdWPage 1137-1235, fdOdonto 1635-1703, FDAES()

//   - LIVERSIDGE (C-F6): no band when an H floor lies above the
//     estimate, and the page says why (fdLPage 1284-1290).
//   - LAMENDIN 1992 / PRINCE-UBELAKER 2002 (D2, D4; C-F2, C-F3), the
//     module rebuilt (2837-2911): the three heights in mm in one form
//     (root height RH, periodontosis, translucency; refusals that
//     teach), P and T computed and shown.  Lamendin always, with SD
//     12.5 = his mean error 10 yr (Table 1) x sqrt(pi/2), in ONE named
//     constant, fdLamSD (D4; v3.8a used 10.0 as the SD).  Prince &
//     Ubelaker when Setup gives the sex and white or black ancestry:
//     their group's equation as printed in the text (pp 111-112), with
//     RH, and the Table's root mean square error as its SEE (Tables
//     3-6).  Its estimate is always shown; it is used (band, pool) only
//     inside P-U's sample, 25 to 99 yr (p 108), and labelled an
//     extrapolation outside it.  Where Lamendin supplies the band the
//     page says his estimate is never below 25.53 yr, so P(age >= 18)

//     intercepts and SDs (29.69 / 27.21 /
//     26.50; 11.1 / 10.8 / 10.5; "Unknown" 27.00 / 12.5) were in

//     Lamendin, and says which; the pool is offered ONE of the two
//     (one tooth).  Pool label "P-U 2002" (purple on the forest plot).
//   - SETUP (D2): ancestry "Unknown", "White", "Black", "Other" (P-U's
//     terms; their sample had no other groups); anc 0..3 (2600-2629).
//   - BANG & RAMM 1970 (D3; C-F4), rebuilt on their own method (3013-
//     3098): intact root or section; the tooth (twelve), then the side
//     or the molar's root (B&R's molar rows serve any of the six upper
//     or six lower molars, p 19), mapped to one table row by data
//     lists; X = TM, the mean of the shortest and longest extent of
//     transparency from the apex (p 4), in mm (0 to 25; an age above
//     110 refused; above 14 mm a warning); one value per root, so the
//     same root twice is refused; X <= 9.0 mm Table VIII / IX, 2nd degree, above 9.0
//     Table X / XI, 1st degree, each row's SA; the page shows the
//     table, its page, the coefficients, A, SA, the 68 % / 95 % lines
//     and B&R's Table XII interval, and near 9.0 mm what the other
//     equation gives.  Several roots of one person: "Another root,
//     same person?", then A = their mean and S_A = (1/n) sqrt(sum
//     Sa^2 + 85 n(n-1)) (p 21), and only that one value goes to the
//     pool; with a molar root or an upper first premolar among them
//     the page gives B&R's advice to leave those out (p 19).  v3.8a's
//     "14.8 + 1.35 T%" line was in no paper: gone.
//   - JOHANSON (C-F5; D5 open): 8.98T (Table 5) stays.  The menu and
//     page name the spreads: "SD 5.16 yr = SD of Johanson's fit,
//     Table 5 (46 people, 1-11 teeth each)" -- 46 INDIVIDUALS, 162 teeth

// WHAT CHANGED IN v3.8a  (17 Oct 2026; line numbers are v3.8's)
//   Five display fixes from the emulator run of v3.8 and the "= <"
//   pass (Roger approved the pass, 17 Oct 2026, 21:18).  No age, SD
//   or interval changes; a probability changes only where the
//   Prime's 12 digits had rounded it to exactly 0 or 1.
//   - NO "=" BEFORE A RELATION.  fdPct writes "< 0.1" or "> 99.9"
//     at its floor, and four displays put that after "= ": the
//     verdict band read "P(age >= 18) = < 0.1%".  New fdEq returns
//     " = 50.0", or " < 0.1" / " > 99.9" when the relation is
//     already there, for the band (fdBand, 638), the posterior
//     panel's P(age >= t) and P(age < t) (fdPost, 1844, 1846) and
//     the summary returned to Home (3394).  Left alone: the M3
//     page's "P(age >= 18) = " lines (2708-2709, 2715-2716), whose
//     table values (6.1 to 93.9 %) never reach the floor, and the
//     lines that put a colon before fdPct.
//   - LOWER TAILS EVERYWHERE.  v3.8 computed P(age >= 18) for
//     Willems and Liversidge as the lower tail NORMALD_CDF(m, s,
//     2m - 18); everything else still used 1 - NORMALD_CDF(m, s,
//     t), which is exactly 0 in 12 digits once the tail is below
//     about 5E-13.  The emulator's posterior (Willems 11.74 / 0.9 +
//     Liversidge 8.66 / 1.40: mean 10.84, SD 0.95) printed "P(age
//     >= 16): < 0.1%" above "P(age >= 18): 0.0%", and the band
//     said "= 0.0%".  Now the lower tail is used for the threshold
//     table (1714), the posterior panel (1842, 1848-1850),
//     Lamendin, Johanson and Bang & Ramm (2790, 2889, 2975) and the
//     posterior result (3062-3064), and each P(age < t) is a CDF
//     of its own (1715, 1846), not 100 - P.
//   - EXACT 0 OR 100 FROM A NORMAL MODEL.  A normal tail is never
//     exactly 0 or 1, so new fdPctN prints an exact 0 as "< 0.1"
//     and an exact 100 as "> 99.9" (P(age < 18) for that child is
//     0.99999999999998, which 12 digits hold as 1).  Every
//     normal-model probability goes through it: the band, the
//     threshold table, the posterior panel, the posterior and
//     report pages and the summary (638, 1717, 1844-1850,
//     3077-3079, 3104, 3394).  fdPct itself is unchanged: a pool
//     weight of 100 % (one method pooled) is a real 100.0 %, and the
//     M3 page shows its table as printed.
//   - "= Mean of 1 tooth" (fdLPage, 1196), not "1 teeth".
//   - The Willems page that has no age (fewer than seven teeth with
//     a printed score) says "any key or tap = next" (1151, through
//     fdFtX): the Liversidge page follows it.
//   - The odontogram's Willems mark (1617-1619) starts 2 px lower,
//     post 193-201 and cap 193-194, clear of line 5 of the panel.
//
// WHAT CHANGED IN v3.8  (17 Oct 2026; line numbers are v3.7b's)
//   The Willems module is rebuilt on the published tables.  A check
//   of v3.7b against the papers (FDAES_Willems_Check_2026-10-17_v1.md)
//   found that its "Willems" table was a table of per-tooth AGES for
//   six teeth that is not in Willems (2001), and that it ran about
//   1.9 years older than Liversidge (2006) -- up to about 5 years
//   for M1 and I2.  Roger, 17 Oct 2026, 20:19: "do 3) both please":
//   Willems as published for the result, and Liversidge tooth by
//   tooth on the odontogram.
//   - WILLEMS (2001) AS PUBLISHED (new: fdTabs, fdWSc, fdWSum,
//     fdWEst, fdWPage).  All SEVEN left mandibular teeth, 31-37; the
//     boys' and girls' score tables exactly as printed (Tables 1 and
//     2, p 894, the negative scores included); the seven scores are
//     SUMMED, and the sum is the dental age.  Its uncertainty is the
//     paper's own test of the method (p 894): boys mean error 0.0,
//     s.d. 0.9 yr; girls mean 0.2, s.d. 1.3 yr.  The mean error is
//     stated on the page, not subtracted.  Sex unknown: both sums and
//     their 50:50 mixture (mean = the average of the two sums; SD =
//     sqrt((0.9^2 + 1.3^2)/2 + (difference/2)^2); P(age >= 18) = the
//     average of the two sexes' P).  No number unless all seven teeth
//     are staged at stages Willems scored: the page says which tooth
//     is missing or unscored.  The invented SE heuristic (wbse and
//     the 0.07 interpolation, 1842-1844 and 859-860) is gone, with
//     the old wm / wf age tables (1788-1803) and fdWAge (614-629).
//   - LIVERSIDGE (2006) TOOTH BY TOOTH (new: fdLvMn, fdLvSD, fdLvH,
//     fdLvEst, fdLPage).  For each staged tooth, the mean age (and
//     SD) of the children IN that stage, Table X: girls, boys, or
//     girls and boys when the sex is unknown.  At H, Table X has no
//     figure; the tooth shows "at least" Table IX's mean age of
//     attaining H (the girls' value when the sex is unknown: the
//     earlier, so the bound stays a lower bound).  The teeth below H
//     are averaged, with two SEs: if the teeth were independent,
//     sqrt(sum SD^2)/k; if they move together, (sum SD)/k.
//     P(age >= 18) uses the second, the larger.
//   - THE ODONTOGRAM (fdOdonto, 737-958) stages SEVEN teeth; 31 is a
//     normal tile now.  A staged tile shows its stage letter and
//     Liversidge's in-stage mean ("35 F 11.0"); at H, ">=" and Table
//     IX's age as printed (two decimals, so the bound is never rounded
//     up); a dash where Table X has no figure (an early stage).
//     The panel: the selected tooth's label, its Liversidge figure in
//     words, the running Liversidge estimate with both SEs, the "at
//     least" of any H teeth, and Willems's sum once all seven have a
//     printed score.  On the age axis: Liversidge in blue (+/- the
//     larger SE), Willems as a magenta mark.  Taps, Up/Down, Enter,
//     +, Esc, Clear, Cancel and Done work as before.
//   - AFTER DONE: the pages "WILLEMS (2001): the published method"
//     and "LIVERSIDGE (2006): tooth by tooth", then the pool
//     question, which offers only what was computed and a teaching
//     page, "Two ways to read the same teeth" (fdTwoWays; also in
//     About).  One answer at most is pooled, since both methods read
//     the same teeth: Willems with the paper's s.d. (the mixture's SD
//     with the sex unknown), Liversidge with the larger SE.  The pool,
//     the posterior page and the forest plot name the entry "Willems
//     2001" or "Liversidge 2006" (fdForest, 1231, 1271-1275 and 1290:
//     their colours, and a label column that widens only when a name
//     is longer than nine characters).
//   - P(age >= 18) for both methods is computed as a lower tail,
//     NORMALD_CDF(m, s, 2m - 18), equal to 1 - NORMALD_CDF(m, s, 18)
//     but not rounded to 1 - 1 = 0 in the Prime's 12 digits when it is
//     tiny, as it is for most children.  New fdF1 / fdF2 print one or
//     two fixed decimals that can never turn scientific.
//   - The header: PROVENANCE CONFIRMED (v3.8) replaces the two
//     PROVENANCE NOT CONFIRMED items on the Willems tables and the SE
//     heuristic (353-359); REFERENCES give Willems in full and add
//     Liversidge (2006).  The self-check (2420-2425) now sums Willems
//     at G (11.74) and works a Liversidge example (new page 4).
//     About adds a "v3.8" page and the teaching page after the
//     misprint pages, and rewords the two lines of "v3.6 NEW
//     SCREENS" and "v3.6 NOTES" that described the six-tooth version
//     (2506-2507, 2523-2524).
//   - NOT changed: M3, Lamendin / Prince-Ubelaker, Johanson, Bang &
//     Ramm, Setup, the main menus, the Bayesian arithmetic and its
//     floor, the verdict band's 0.83 / 0.58 cut-points, the misprint
//     pages, the stage labels and pictures.
//
// WHAT CHANGED IN v3.7b  (17 Oct 2026; line numbers are v3.7a's)

//   change labels to Demirjian's wording".  The stage LETTERS, every
//   table, every verdict and every computed age are unchanged.
//   - The eight labels, the same strings in all three places they
//     appear: the odontogram's list odStg (683-687) and its Enter
//     menu (843-853), and the M3 grade menu (1812-1816, D-H):
//       A  initial calcification  ->  A  cusp tips, not joined
//       B  coalescence of cusps   ->  B  cusp tips joined
//       C  complete cusp outline  ->  C  occlusal enamel complete
//       D  crown 1/2 complete     ->  D  crown complete to CEJ
//       E  crown complete         ->  E  root shorter than crown
//       F  root 1/3 formed        ->  F  root >= crown, apex wide
//       G  root 2/3 formed        ->  G  canal walls parallel
//       H  apex closed            ->  H  apex closed
//     None is longer than 27 characters: the CHOOSE box cuts a line
//     after about 30 (emulator, round 2), and the odontogram panel's
//     line (the tooth's name, then its label) must fit 305 px.  For
//     that the panel's separator is now ": " (750), one space less.
//   - The stage pictures (fdPicto, 608-647) are redrawn to match:
//     A two cusp tips; B the tips joined; C the occlusal half of the
//     crown; D the whole crown and a root just begun (3 rows); E a
//     root shorter than the crown (8 rows < 12); F a root longer
//     than the crown (14 rows) whose end flares open, a funnel; G
//     the full root (18 rows), walls parallel, apex open; H the full
//     root with the apex closed.

// FIXES FROM v3.3 (made in v3.4; all verified off-device, NONE
// tested on hardware yet)
//   [CRITICAL] Willems 2nd-screen stage index was +5, must be +6.
//     Screen 2 returns 1=F 2=G 3=H; downstream wsi := wsc-1 indexes
//     columns A=1..H=8, so F must arrive as 7. With +5 EVERY stage read
//     one column early: a male I2 staged F returned 8.6 yr (stage E)
//     instead of 11.2 yr. Silent, no error raised.
//   [CRITICAL] CHOOSE index variables were not reset to 0 before the
//     call. CHOOSE does not modify its index on CANCEL, so cancelling
//     re-used the PREVIOUS answer. Worst case: ch_yn drives "Add to
//     Bayesian pool?" six times - answer Yes once, cancel a later
//     prompt, and the estimate is silently added a second time.
//     All 15 call sites now initialise the index first.
//   [CRITICAL] INPUT return value was discarded. Cancelling left the
//     variables at 0, so Lamendin computed 0.18(0)+0.42(0)+25.53 and
//     stored 25.53 yr - the bare intercept - as a real estimate.
//     All INPUT calls now use the list-of-names form and test the
//     return; the six Johanson prompts collapse into one form.
//   [STATS] Precision floor added to the Bayesian posterior. See below.
//   Display: all STRING(ROUND(x,d)) -> fixed-decimal STRING(x,2,d);
//     probabilities through fdPct(), which floors at "< 0.1" so a
//     posterior below display resolution never prints as "0.0%".
//   Cancelling the Willems 2nd screen now skips the tooth instead of
//     indexing column 11 of an 8-column row.
//
// CORRECTIONS TO THE v3.3 HEADER NOTES:
//   - CHOOSE simple syntax caps at 14 options, not 7. The two-screen
//     menu split is a readability choice, not a platform requirement.
//   - INPUT DOES support the 4-6 argument form in programs. It is the
//     device-verified idiom (first argument must be a LIST of variable
//     NAMES, written back in place) and is used in 336 programs in the
//     local corpus. v3.3's 2-argument form is what discarded the
//     cancel return.
//
// HOW TO ENTER:
//   Connectivity Kit (or Apps -> Program Editor -> New -> Name: FDAES).
//   Paste the WHOLE file, from the #pragma line at the top to the
//   final END; - the pragma must stay on line 1.
//   Press [Check] to compile, then run FDAES() from the Program
//   Catalog.

// ARITHMETIC SELF-CHECK (independent Python cross-check,
// verify_FDAES_v3.9a.py Part K; v3.9's worked examples):
//   One tooth of a white male, RH 15, periodontosis 3, translucency
//     6 mm: P = 3 x 100 / 15 = 20, T = 6 x 100 / 15 = 40;
//     Lamendin 0.18 x 20 + 0.42 x 40 + 25.53 = 45.93 yr, SD 12.5;
//     P-U, white males, 0.15 x 15 + 0.29 x 20 + 0.39 x 40 + 23.17 =
//     46.82 yr, SEE 9.576177 (Table 6).
//   Johanson A = 1, S = 1.5, P = 0.5, C = 0.5, R = 0, T = 2.5 (2+
//     teeth): 45.985 yr, SD 5.16.
//   Bang & Ramm, one person: upper left second premolar, section, X =
//     6 mm (Table IX row 9): 22.34 + 7.59 x 6 - 0.393 x 36 = 53.732
//     yr, SA 6.88; lower left lateral incisor, intact, X = 11 mm
//     (Table X row 13): 33.65 + 3.53 x 11 = 72.48 yr, SA 11.12; the
//     two combined (p 21): A = 63.106 yr, S_A = (1/2) sqrt(6.88^2 +
//     11.12^2 + 85 x 2 x 1) = 9.233 yr (6.538 without the 85).
//   Bayesian, P-U + Johanson + Bang & Ramm (the combined value):
//     precisions 0.01090 + 0.03756 + 0.01173 = 0.06019; mean 49.47 yr;
//     SD 4.076 x 1.25 = 5.09, floored to 5.16; weights 18.1 / 62.4 /
//     19.5 %.
//   All confirmed to the stated decimals, and shown by the program on
//   the Verify Calculations pages beside its own computation.
//   M3 (v3.7): the 20 values of Mincer 1993 Table 4 as corrected by
//     the erratum, checked one by one against page images of the
//     paper and the erratum (verify_FDAES_v3.7.py).
//   Willems 2001 (v3.8), all seven teeth at G: boys 2.07 + 1.32 +
//     1.09 + 2.43 + 0.40 + 1.95 + 2.48 = 11.74 yr; girls 3.19 + 0.79
//     + 1.72 + 1.58 + 0.55 + 1.82 + 2.09 = 11.74 yr.  All seven at
//     H: boys 16.03, girls 15.79 yr.
//   Liversidge 2006 (v3.8), a boy with I2 at F, C at G, P1 at E:
//     in-stage means 7.49, 12.02, 8.35 -> 27.86 / 3 = 9.29 yr;
//     SE if independent sqrt(0.95^2 + 1.33^2 + 1.05^2) / 3 = 0.65 yr;
//     SE if they move together (0.95 + 1.33 + 1.05) / 3 = 1.11 yr.
//     (verify_FDAES_v3.8.py; both on the Verify Calculations pages.)
//
// PROVENANCE CONFIRMED (v3.7): THE M3 TABLE.
//   Mincer HH, Harris EF, Berryman HE.  The A.B.F.O. study of third
//   molar development and its use as an estimator of chronological
//   age.  J Forensic Sci 1993;38(2):379-390.  Table 4 (p 384):
//   "Empirical probabilities (%) of an individual being at least 18
//   years of age based on the grade of third molar formation."
//   Footnote: "Values are based just on whites.  Probabilities for
//   the terminal grade (H) presume that, based on other criteria,
//   the subject is less than 25 years of age."
//   - The sample: 823 ABFO cases aged 14.1-24.9, from the US and
//     Canada; Table 4 uses whites only.  The probabilities come from
//     normal-curve theory, with each grade's mean age and SD (the
//     paper's "Prediction of Age 18" section).
//   - The values used here are the journal's CORRECTED table: J
//     Forensic Sci 1993, p 1524, "In the March 1993 issue, a
//     misprint occurred.  Table 4 of Mincer et al. p. 384 should
//     have appeared as follows."  The only change: mandible, males,
//     grade E, printed 69.4, corrected to 29.4.
//   - The authors' own reading (p 387): grades A-D, "little
//     likelihood" of being 18; E, F and G, "essentially a coin toss
//     (50:50)"; H, "reasonably confident" of at least 18.  Grade H
//     occurs in all mature third molars regardless of age, so the H
//     figures assume other evidence excludes anyone over 24.
//   - The uncorrected 69.4 was reprinted in Harris, Mincer, Anderson
//     & Senn (2010), ch 13, Table 13.12, p 289 (confirmed 19 Oct 2026
//     from Roger's copy of the book, H:\Books and Papers\Forensic
//     Dentistry, Second Edition.pdf; the author manuscript numbers it
//     Table 12), and survives in copies of the paper that lack the
//     erratum page (see the misprint page, fdM3Hist).
//   Read from page images of Roger's own copy of the paper and of the
//   erratum page, 17 Oct 2026.
//
// STAGE WORDING (v3.7b): DEMIRJIAN, GOLDSTEIN & TANNER (1973).
//   Willems (2001) and Mincer et al. (1993) both use the eight
//   stages of Demirjian, Goldstein & Tanner (1973).  From v3.7b the
//   stage labels (odStg, the odontogram's Enter menu, the M3 grade
//   menu) and the stage pictures (fdPicto) follow them.  For the
//   molars:
//     A  calcification of single occlusal points (the cusp tips),
//        without fusion
//     B  the calcified points fused; the occlusal outline regular
//     C  enamel complete at the occlusal surface; dentine deposit
//        begun; the outline of the pulp chamber curved
//     D  crown complete down to the cemento-enamel junction (CEJ);
//        root formation begun
//     E  root length LESS than the crown height
//     F  root length EQUAL TO OR GREATER than the crown height; the
//        root ends funnel-shaped (apex wide open)
//     G  walls of the root canal parallel; apex still partly open
//     H  apex completely closed; periodontal membrane of uniform
//        width around the root and the apex
//   The menu labels condense these to fit the CHOOSE box (at most 27
//   characters; the box cuts a line after about 30).  Before v3.7b
//   the labels were this program's own (A initial calcification, B
//   coalescence of cusps, C complete cusp outline, D crown 1/2
//   complete, E crown complete, F root 1/3 formed, G root 2/3
//   formed, H apex closed) and did not match Demirjian at D-G;
//   Roger decided on 17 Oct 2026 to use Demirjian's wording.  The
//   definitions above were checked on 17 Oct 2026 against the text of
//   Roger's copy of the paper ("Dental Formation Stages", Appendix,
//   pp. 221-226; H:\21st AgeEst Wkshp\Age estimation\Child DAE\
//   Demirjian et al - 1973.pdf), where G and H refer to the distal
//   root in molars and D's root begins "in the form of a spicule".
//
// PROVENANCE CONFIRMED (v3.8): THE WILLEMS AND LIVERSIDGE TABLES.
//   Willems G, Van Olmen A, Spiessens B, Carels C.  Dental age
//   estimation in Belgian children: Demirjian's technique revisited.
//   J Forensic Sci 2001;46(4):893-895.
//   - Tables 1 (boys) and 2 (girls), p 894: a score "expressed
//     directly in years" for each of the seven left mandibular teeth
//     at each stage A-H, and "..." where none was given.  "The sum of
//     the values from these tables corresponding to the observed
//     developmental stages of the seven left mandibular teeth
//     directly results in the estimated dental age."  The negative
//     girls' scores (P1 A -0.95 and B -0.15, P2 A -0.19) come from
//     the weighted ANOVA and "are only meaningful in the total formula
//     summing the maturity scores of all seven left mandibular teeth".
//   - The test of the adapted method on a second sample (p 894):
//     overestimation for boys median 0.1, mean 0.0, s.d. 0.9 yr; for
//     girls median 0.2, mean 0.2, s.d. 1.3 yr.  FDAES uses the s.d.
//     as the method's SE and states the mean without subtracting it.
//   - The sample: Belgian Caucasian children from Leuven (boys 1.8 to
//     18.0 yr, girls 2.1 to 18.0).  The paper gives no rule for a
//     missing tooth, so FDAES gives no Willems age without all seven.
//   - In fdTabs as fdWb and fdWg.  Read from the page image of p 894
//     of Roger's copy (H:\21st AgeEst Wkshp\Age estimation\Child DAE\
//     Willems et al - 2001.pdf), 17 Oct 2026.  The PDF's text layer
//     drops the three minus signs; the image has them.
//   Liversidge HM, Chaillet N, Mornstad H, Nystrom M, Rowlings K,
//   Taylor J, Willems G.  Timing of Demirjian's tooth formation
//   stages.  Ann Hum Biol 2006;33(4):454-470.
//   - 9,002 children aged 2 to 16.99 from eight countries, Willems's
//     Leuven sample among them.
//   - Table X (p 467): "Mean ages of children 'in a tooth formation
//     stage'" -- mean, SE, SD and n for girls, boys, and girls and
//     boys.  It omits stage H: "it is not possible to predict how
//     much time has passed since the child entered this stage."  In
//     fdTabs as fdLgM / fdLgS (girls), fdLbM / fdLbS (boys) and
//     fdLcM / fdLcS (girls and boys), the means and the SDs.
//   - Table IX (p 465): the mean age of ATTAINING each stage, girls
//     and boys.  FDAES uses its H row only, and only as "at least"
//     (fdLhG, fdLhB).
//   - Read from page images of pp. 465 and 467 of Roger's copy
//     (H:\Age Est Papers\06_Liversidge...pdf), 17 Oct 2026.
//   The check that found v3.7b's tables unsourced, and how far they
//   ran from Liversidge: FDAES_Willems_Check_2026-10-17_v1.md.
//
// PROVENANCE NOT CONFIRMED  (arithmetic is self-consistent; the SOURCE
// of these numbers has NOT been checked against the papers):
//   - (v3.8: the two items that stood here -- v3.7b's Willems age
//     tables, which were not Willems's, and its invented SE heuristic
//     -- are resolved; see PROVENANCE CONFIRMED (v3.8) above.)
//   - (v3.7b: the Willems STAGE WORDING item that stood here -- the
//     program's own labels, not Demirjian's at D-G -- is resolved;
//     see STAGE WORDING above.)
//   - (v3.9: the Bang & Ramm item that stood here -- a linear T% line
//     that was not their method -- and the Prince & Ubelaker item --
//     intercepts that were in neither paper -- are resolved: both
//     methods are implemented as published; see PROVENANCE CONFIRMED
//     (v3.9) below.)
//   Nothing stands here now.  Every number FDAES uses has been read
//   from page images of the papers; the help topics name the pages.
//
// PROVENANCE CONFIRMED (v3.9): PRINCE & UBELAKER AND BANG & RAMM.
//   Read from 300-dpi page images of Roger's copies (fdaes39/pages:
//   P-U pp 111-115, B&R pp 20-24) by two transcribers working
//   independently (fdaes39/tr), checked against the OCR and against
//   the tables' own arithmetic, and reconciled into
//   fdaes39/PU_tables_v1.csv and BR_tables_v1.csv (with
//   PU_BR_tables_v1_NOTES.md): all 528 Bang & Ramm cells and every
//   Prince & Ubelaker estimate, SE, t, RMSE and n agree.  fdPUTab and
//   fdBRTab were generated from those files and carry their own notes
//   (what is printed oddly; how to check a row by hand).
//   - Prince & Ubelaker: each Table's "Prediction Profile" means put
//     into its own estimates give its printed "Mean of Response"
//     (47.7600, 56.9500, 52.1000, 54.1010).  The text's equations
//     round those estimates; FDAES uses the text's.
//   - Bang & Ramm: each quadratic and its linear twin were fitted to
//     the same teeth (n agrees in all 24 rows); every B2 sign was
//     checked against its linear twin (a flipped sign moves a row by
//     50-114 years).  The several-roots formula (p 21) was read the
//     same way by both transcribers; its 85 is derived on p 11.
//
// WHY GUSTAFSON 1950 IS EXCLUDED (v3.9a: read from Roger's copies,
// 19 Oct 2026; FDAES_source_facts_v1a.md section 9):
//   - Gustafson (1950): each of six changes is "allotted 0, 1, 2 and
//     3 points" (p 47), and Formula 1 sums them, A + P + S + C + R +
//     T = points (p 49): the six weigh the same.  "41 teeth in all
//     were examined"; y = 11.43 + 4.56x, r 0.98 (p 51); "the error of
//     estimation" +/- 3.63 yr (p 51).  37 teeth examined twice by
//     "the observer": a single examination's error +/- 0.74 points,
//     +/- 3.37 yr (p 52); no second observer is described.
//   - Maples & Rice (1979): the scores are "semiquantitative
//     (ordinal)" (p 168); the 19 teeth that first built the line were
//     among the 41 that formalized it and then tested it, "circular
//     reasoning" (pp 168-169); they judged the line fitted to the
//     estimated ages of Gustafson's Table 5, not the real ones (pp
//     169-170) -- an inference: their own refit of those ages (y =
//     4.46x + 11.56) does not give 4.56 and 11.43 either.  Refitted
//     on the real ages (y = 4.26x + 13.45) the standard error of the
//     estimate is +/- 7.03 yr (p 170).  They took 3.63 for an
//     average deviation (p 169), "not the correct statistic".
//   - Lucy & Pollard (1995): the line is "impossible to recalculate"
//     (p 223); 3.63 is not an average deviation of Gustafson's data
//     (5.34 or 8.63 yr, p 224); age regressed on points is "the
//     wrong way round" (p 224).
//   - Aykroyd (1995, letter, p 925): 3.63 is the prediction SD for a
//     future MEAN, computed with the sample SD of the ages (16.96)
//     where the regression's SD belonged; the correct average
//     prediction SD is 7.2.  3.63 is not the error of one estimate.
//     (v3.9a said "a mean deviation", after Maples & Rice: v3.9b.)
//   - Lamendin et al. (1992, p 1377): "On a sample of 39 individuals
//     (42 to 79 years of age), the ME using the Lamendin method was
//     significantly lower than with the method of Gustafson (8.9 +/-
//     2.2 versus 14.2 +/- 3.4) (P < 0.05)" -- a MEAN ERROR of 14.2 yr
//     for Gustafson's method, 8.9 for Lamendin's (v3.9; v3.8a called
//     14.2 an "SE" from a "39-tooth test": the abstract says 39
//     teeth, the text and Fig. 3 say 39 individuals).
//   - Lucy et al. (1996, p 189): the data are "ordinal or
//     categorical", and regression is "not particularly appropriate"
//     to them.
//   (v3.9 listed these without sources, marked "not checked"; it also
//   said "no inter-rater reliability" and "textbook standard for ~50
//   years".  Gustafson's repeat study is one observer's (p 52); the
//   50 years has no source here.  v3.9a drops both.)
//
// LAMENDIN'S AND JOHANSON'S ERRORS (v3.9), read from page images of
// the papers, 19 Oct 2026:
//   Lamendin H, Baccino E, Humbert JF, Tavernier JC, Nossintchouk
//   RM, Zerilli A (1992) J Forensic Sci 37(5):1373-1379.
//   - The equation (p 1375): A = 0.18 P + 0.42 T + 25.53, with P =
//     periodontosis height x 100 / root height and T = transparency
//     height x 100 / root height, measured on the labial surface.
//     (The text on p 1376 calls the constant 25.33; the equation and
//     the abstract print 25.53, which FDAES uses.)
//   - Table 1 (p 1376): mean error (ME) by decade, 24.8 yr at 26-29
//     down to 6.3 at 60-69 and 18.9 at 80-89; 10 yr over all 306
//     teeth.  Table 2 (p 1377): 8.4 yr on 45 teeth from 24 forensic
//     cases.  "The mean error (ME) is the average difference between
//     actual and estimated age" (Table 2's caption).  Whether the signs
//     were ignored is not stated; FDAES treats it as the average size
//     of a miss (a mean ABSOLUTE error), as Prince & Ubelaker's "mean
//     difference" is; it is not an SD.  For normal errors SD = ME x
//     sqrt(pi/2) =
//     1.2533 x ME = 12.5: FDAES's SD since v3.9 (fdLamSD; Roger's D4,
//     19 Oct 2026; v3.8a used 10 itself).  Prince & Ubelaker's groups
//     bear the ratio out (RMSE / mean absolute difference 1.28-1.32).
//   Johanson G (1971) Age determinations from human teeth.  Odontologisk
//     Revy 22, Suppl 21 (no page range on Roger's copy).
//   - Table 5, line VI: Age = 11.02 + 8.98T + 5.14A + 4.14P + 5.57R
//     + 3.71C + 2.30S.  The running text beside it ("VI. If the last
//     and sixth variable S is used ...") prints the same formula
//     with 9.98T.  FDAES uses Table 5's 8.98.
//   - "Standard deviation = 5.16", printed under VI: the residual SD
//     of that fit.  Its sum of squares runs i = 1 ... 46, and "the
//     whole material consists of 46 individuals" (162 teeth from 46
//     people, one to eleven teeth each; "Material"): 46 PEOPLE.  His
//     procedure takes the mean value when more than one tooth is used
//     ("If more than one tooth is used the mean value is taken"), and
//     "the 'mouth' (2 teeth or more) has been the entity instead of
//     single teeth".  (The fleet review and the v3.9 brief said 46
//     teeth and "one tooth's SD"; the paper says individuals.)
//   - Table 8 prints an "Error" by the number of teeth: 6.0 for one,
//     5.2 for two, 4.9, 4.7, then 4.6 down to 4.4 for ten (what kind
//     of error is not stated; Johanson calls his error figures standard
//     deviations, and 5.2 sits beside the fit's 5.16).  Since v3.9a
//     FDAES's SD for "1 tooth" is Table 8's 6.0 (fdJoh1SD; Roger's D5,
//     19 Oct 2026); v3.9 offered 8.00, its own allowance.  Lucy,
//     Pollard & Roberts (1995) tested the method on 17 other people:
//     single roots missed by 4.5 yr on average (SD about 5.6).
//   - His worked example of the procedure uses 8.98T too (44.92 yr).
//
// REFERENCES:
//   Willems G, Van Olmen A, Spiessens B, Carels C (2001) Dental age
//     estimation in Belgian children: Demirjian's technique
//     revisited.  J Forensic Sci 46(4):893-895  [Tables 1-2, p 894:
//     the scores summed by the Willems module]
//   Liversidge HM, Chaillet N, Mornstad H, Nystrom M, Rowlings K,
//     Taylor J, Willems G (2006) Timing of Demirjian's tooth
//     formation stages.  Ann Hum Biol 33(4):454-470  [Tables IX and
//     X, pp. 465-467: the per-tooth ages on the odontogram]
//   Demirjian, Goldstein & Tanner (1973) Hum Biol 45(2):211-227
//     [the stages A-H used by Willems, Liversidge and Mincer et al.]
//   Lamendin H, Baccino E, Humbert JF, Tavernier JC, Nossintchouk RM,
//     Zerilli A (1992) A simple technique for age estimation in adult
//     corpses: the two criteria dental method.  J Forensic Sci
//     37(5):1373-1379
//   Prince DA, Ubelaker DH (2002) Application of Lamendin's adult
//     dental aging technique to a diverse skeletal sample.  J Forensic
//     Sci 47(1):107-116  [v3.9: the paper's own reference line, p 107;
//     v3.8a said 47(3):519-523]
//   Johanson G (1971) Age determinations from human teeth: a critical
//     evaluation with special consideration of changes after fourteen
//     years of age.  Odontologisk Revy, Volume 22, Supplement 21.
//     Lund: CWK Gleerup.  [the cover of Roger's copy; it prints no
//     page range (Lamendin 1992's reference list gives pp 40-126).
//     v3.8a said "22(Suppl 21):1-126"]
//   Bang G, Ramm E (1970) Determination of age in humans from root
//     dentin transparency.  Acta Odontologica Scandinavica, Vol. 28,
//     1970, pp. 3-35, as Lamendin 1992's reference list cites it
//     (p 1378).  [Roger's copy prints the title, the authors and
//     folios 4-35 but no journal line, so neither the journal nor an
//     issue number can be confirmed from it; v3.8a said "28(1):3-35"]
//   Mincer et al. (1993) J Forensic Sci 38(2):379-390
//   Erratum, J Forensic Sci 1993, p 1524 ("In the March 1993 issue, a
//     misprint occurred"; the page prints no volume; v3.8a said
//     "38:1524").  It corrects Mincer et al. Table 4: mandible, males,
//     grade E is 29.4, not 69.4.  (fdM3Hist's page says "vol 38, p
//     1524": the erratum page itself prints no volume; 38 is inferred
//     from the paper's own volume, 38(2):379-390.)
//   Harris EF, Mincer HH, Anderson KM, Senn DR (2010) Age estimation
//     from oral and dental structures.  Ch 13 in Senn DR, Stimson PG
//     (eds), Forensic Dentistry, 2nd ed.  Boca Raton: CRC Press.
//     [Table 13.12, p 289, reprints Mincer's Table 4 WITHOUT the

//     19 Oct 2026 from Roger's copy of the book, H:\Books and

//     Harris_Mincer_Anderson_Senn_2010_ForensicDentistry_ch13.pdf)

// LAYOUT NOTE (fdPage)
//   Font 2 is about 8 px per character, so a line that starts at
//   x = 6 with a 308-px clip width holds about 38 characters.  Every
//   fixed line below is kept to 37 or fewer, and every TEXTOUT_P of
//   variable text passes the width so nothing runs off the edge.
//   The drawn screens of v3.6 compose every frame in G1, blit once,
//   then paint their soft keys with DRAWMENU (a tap with y >= 218 is
//   slot MIN(6, IP(x/53.4)+1)); hardware keys stay as shortcuts.
//   v3.9: a page with a verdict band holds 12 lines (the last at row
//   184, ending near 197), the band takes rows 199-226 in two lines
//   (font 2 at row 200, font 1 at row 214) and the footer sits at row
//   228 (224 on every other page).
// ============================================================

// v3.7a: a one-off footer for the last page of the next fdPageV
// call ("" = the usual "back"/"next"); fdPageV clears it after use.
LOCAL fdFtX;

// v3.8: the published tables of the Willems / Liversidge module, set
// once by fdTabs() at INITIALISE and read only through fdWSc, fdLvMn,
// fdLvSD and fdLvH.  Each of the first eight is a FLAT list of 56
// numbers: 7 rows (teeth 31, 32, ... 37) of 8 columns (stages A, B,
// ... H), so tooth ti (1..7) at stage st (1..8) is entry (ti-1)*8+st.
//   fdWb, fdWg     Willems 2001 Tables 1 (boys), 2 (girls): SCORES
//   fdLbM, fdLbS   Liversidge 2006 Table X, boys: mean age in the
//                  stage, and its SD
//   fdLgM, fdLgS   the same for girls
//   fdLcM, fdLcS   the same for girls and boys together
//   fdLhB, fdLhG   Liversidge 2006 Table IX: the mean age of
//                  ATTAINING stage H, boys and girls (7 numbers each)
LOCAL fdWb, fdWg, fdLbM, fdLbS, fdLgM, fdLgS;
LOCAL fdLcM, fdLcS, fdLhB, fdLhG;
// v3.9: the Prince & Ubelaker equations and the Bang & Ramm tables,
// set once by fdPUTab() and fdBRTab() at INITIALISE (their comments
// say where every number came from)
LOCAL fdPUc, fdPUr, fdPUn, fdBR8, fdBR9, fdBR10;
LOCAL fdBR11, fdBRn;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// Percentage formatter. Non-EXPORT helper, defined before first use
// so it does not appear in the program catalog.
// STRING mode 1 = Standard DROPS trailing zeros regardless of the
// digits argument, so 50.0 printed "50" and 7.10 printed "7.1" and the
// decimal width drifted line to line. Mode 2 = Fixed pins it.
// The floor matters here specifically: a posterior probability below
// 0.05 % would print "0.0%", which in a forensic report reads as
// IMPOSSIBLE. It is not impossible, merely below display resolution.
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdPct(pv)
BEGIN
  IF pv > 0 AND pv < 0.1 THEN RETURN "< 0.1"; END;
  IF pv < 100 AND pv > 99.9 THEN RETURN "> 99.9"; END;
  RETURN STRING(pv,2,1);
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// A probability from a NORMAL model, in percent (v3.8a).  A normal
// tail is never exactly 0 or 1, but the Prime's 12 digits can make
// it so: a tail smaller than about 5E-13 leaves 0.99999999999998,
// which 12 digits hold as 1.  An exact 0 or 100 here therefore means
// "too small to hold", and it prints as fdPct's floor, "< 0.1" or
// "> 99.9", like any tail just inside the floor.  (fdPct itself is
// unchanged: a pool weight of exactly 100 % is a real 100.0.)
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdPctN(pv)
BEGIN
  IF pv <= 0 THEN RETURN "< 0.1"; END;
  IF pv >= 100 THEN RETURN "> 99.9"; END;
  RETURN fdPct(pv);
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// " = 50.0", or " < 0.1" / " > 99.9" when fdPct has already written
// the relation (v3.8a).  "P(age >= 18) = < 0.1%" said two things at
// once; "P(age >= 18) < 0.1%" says the one that is true.  The first
// character decides, and it is read only when there is one (DIM
// first: an empty string has no first character).
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdEq(eqS)
BEGIN
  IF DIM(eqS) > 0 THEN
    IF LEFT(eqS, 1) == "<" OR LEFT(eqS, 1) == ">" THEN RETURN " " + eqS; END;
  END;
  RETURN " = " + eqS;
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// Wait for the finger to lift -- but never for more than a second.
// MOUSE(1) is -1 while nothing touches the screen.  Without the
// time limit a stuck or phantom touch would hold the program here
// for ever, and the only way out would be a reset.  (The ChiTable
// csDrain shape, run on the G2 and G1 on 10 Oct 2026.)
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdDrain()
BEGIN
  LOCAL drT;
  drT := TICKS;                                  // milliseconds
  REPEAT UNTIL MOUSE(1) == -1 OR TICKS - drT > 1000;
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// Wait for any key or tap (drain, wait, drain).  A keypress left
// behind by INPUT or CHOOSE would otherwise satisfy the wait and
// skip the page that was just drawn (Lessons Learned section 7).
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdWaitAny()
BEGIN
  LOCAL waK, waM, waM1;
  REPEAT waK := GETKEY; UNTIL waK == -1;
  fdDrain();
  REPEAT
    waK := GETKEY;
    waM := MOUSE;
    waM1 := waM(1);
  UNTIL waK > -1 OR SIZE(waM1) > 0;
  fdDrain();
  REPEAT waK := GETKEY; UNTIL waK == -1;
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// Verdict band (v3.6; v3.9: symmetric, stated cut-points).  A
// coloured strip that answers the 18-year question for one result.
// Roger's decision D1 (19 Oct 2026): two cut-points, 5 % and 95 %,
// the same distance from each end, printed in the band itself:
//   P(age >= 18) >= 95 %          green  "probably >= 18"
//   P(age >= 18) <= 5 %           red    "probably < 18"
//   anything between              orange "uncertain"
// Both ends are INCLUSIVE: exactly 95 % is green, exactly 5 % red.
// The test is made on P AS PRINTED, rounded to 0.1 % (the band's
// second line shows it), so the colour and the printed number can
// never disagree: a P of 0.94996 prints "95.0%" and is green, 0.9494
// prints "94.9%" and is orange.  The cut-points are bdLo and bdHi
// below, in percent; the band prints them from those two numbers.
// (v3.6 to v3.8a used 0.83 and 0.58, the G and F values of v3.6's
// unsourced M3 ladder; they had no source and are gone.  The M3
// page keeps the authors' own zones, fdZone, below.)
//   vp  P(age >= 18) as a proportion, 0 to 1
//   by  the top row of the band (it is 27 px tall: two lines)
// Line 1 (font 2): the verdict.  Line 2 (font 1): P(age >= 18) and
// the cut-points, e.g. "P(age >= 18) = 97.1%  (cut-points 5% and 95%)".
// Drawn into G1; the caller blits.
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdBand(vp, by)
BEGIN
  LOCAL bdTxt, bdEdge, bdFill, bdPc, bdLo, bdHi;
  bdLo := 5; bdHi := 95;                         // D1: the cut-points, %
  // P as the band prints it: percent, rounded to one decimal
  bdPc := ROUND(vp*100, 1);
  bdTxt := "uncertain"; bdEdge := #FF4500h; bdFill := #FFF0DCh;
  IF bdPc >= bdHi THEN
    bdTxt := ("probably " + CHAR(8805) + " 18"); bdEdge := #008C3Ch; bdFill := #E8F6EBh;
  END;
  IF bdPc <= bdLo THEN
    bdTxt := "probably < 18"; bdEdge := #FF0000h; bdFill := #FFEBEBh;
  END;
  RECT_P(G1, 4, by, 315, by + 27, bdEdge, bdFill);
  TEXTOUT_P(bdTxt, G1, 10, by + 1, 2, bdEdge, 300);
  // v3.8a: fdEq puts " = " before a number, or just a space before
  // the relation fdPct wrote: "P(age >= 18) < 0.1%", never "= < 0.1%";
  // fdPctN: vp comes from a normal model (an exact 0 is "< 0.1")
  TEXTOUT_P(("P(age " + CHAR(8805) + " 18)") + fdEq(fdPctN(vp*100)) + "%  (cut-points " +
            STRING(bdLo,1,0) + "% and " + STRING(bdHi,1,0) + "%)",
            G1, 10, by + 15, 1, bdEdge, 300);
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// M3 zone band (v3.7).  The same strip as fdBand, but it gives the
// AUTHORS' OWN reading of their Table 4 (Mincer, Harris & Berryman
// 1993, p 387), keyed on the GRADE of the third molar rather than
// on a cut-point:
//   zone 1  grades A-D  red     "unlikely to be 18"
//           (paper: "there is little likelihood that he or she is
//           18 years of age")
//   zone 2  grades E-G  orange  "about a coin toss"
//           (paper: "essentially a coin toss (50:50)")
//   zone 3  grade H     green   "reasonably confident >= 18"
//           (paper: "one can be reasonably confident that the
//           subject is indeed at least 18 years of age" -- provided
//           other evidence shows the person is under 25)
// The colours are fdBand's, so red, orange and green mean the same
// thing on every page of the program.
//   zn  the zone, 1 to 3
//   by  the top row of the band (it is 18 px tall)
// Drawn into G1; the caller blits.
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdZone(zn, by)
BEGIN
  LOCAL znTxt, znEdge, znFill;
  // zone 2, the coin toss, unless the grade is D or H
  znTxt := "about a coin toss (grades E-G)";
  znEdge := #FF4500h; znFill := #FFF0DCh;
  IF zn == 1 THEN
    znTxt := "unlikely to be 18 (grades A-D)";
    znEdge := #FF0000h; znFill := #FFEBEBh;
  END;
  IF zn == 3 THEN
    znTxt := ("reasonably confident " + CHAR(8805) + " 18 (grade H)");
    znEdge := #008C3Ch; znFill := #E8F6EBh;
  END;
  RECT_P(G1, 4, by, 315, by + 18, znEdge, znFill);
  TEXTOUT_P(znTxt, G1, 10, by + 3, 2, znEdge, 300);
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// Page viewer.  MSGBOX shows about five lines and silently drops
// the rest, so every result, the report template, the threshold
// table, the self-check and the About pages come through here.
//   ttl  the title, drawn white on an orange band
//   lst  a LIST of line strings; "" gives a blank line
//   vp   P(age >= 18) for a verdict band above the footer (v3.6),
//        or -1 for no band,
//        or (v3.7) -2, -3 or -4 for the M3 page's zone band: the
//        authors' zone 1, 2 or 3 (fdZone), which is -1 - vp
// Thirteen lines fit a page (font 2 at 14 px from y = 30), twelve
// when a band is drawn; a longer list is shown page by page, any
// key or tap moving on.
// A line that begins with "=" or "RESULT" is drawn in magenta,
// the house colour for a derived quantity.  Each page is composed
// in G1 and blitted once, like every other frame in the fleet.
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdPageV(ttl, lst, vp)
BEGIN
  LOCAL pgN, pgPages, pgP, pgFirst, pgLast, pgI;
  LOCAL pgY, pgLine, pgCol, pgFoot, pgPer, pgFy;
  pgN := SIZE(lst);
  pgPer := 13;                                   // 13 lines per page,
  IF vp >= 0 OR vp <= -2 THEN pgPer := 12; END;  // 12 above a band
  pgPages := MAX(1, IP((pgN + pgPer - 1) / pgPer));
  FOR pgP FROM 1 TO pgPages DO
    pgFirst := (pgP - 1) * pgPer + 1;
    pgLast  := MIN(pgN, pgP * pgPer);
    DIMGROB_P(G1, 320, 240, #FFFFFFh);
    // orange title band with the title in white
    RECT_P(G1, 0, 0, 319, 22, #FF4500h, #FF4500h);
    TEXTOUT_P(ttl, G1, 6, 4, 2, #FFFFFFh, 308);
    // the lines of this page
    pgY := 30;
    FOR pgI FROM pgFirst TO pgLast DO
      pgLine := lst(pgI);
      pgCol := #000000h;
      // MID(s, 1, n): a length past the end stops at the end (G2-measured)
      IF DIM(pgLine) > 0 THEN
        IF MID(pgLine, 1, 1) == "=" THEN pgCol := #C800C8h; END;
        IF MID(pgLine, 1, 6) == "RESULT" THEN pgCol := #C800C8h; END;
        TEXTOUT_P(pgLine, G1, 6, pgY, 2, pgCol, 308);
      END;
      pgY := pgY + 14;
    END;
    // the verdict band, rows 199-226 (v3.9: two lines, the second
    // printing the cut-points), below line 12 (which ends near 197)
    IF vp >= 0 THEN fdBand(vp, 199); END;
    // v3.7: or the M3 page's zone band (the authors' reading), one
    // line, rows 201-219 as before
    IF vp <= -2 THEN fdZone(-1 - vp, 201); END;
    // footer: what the next key does, and the page count if paged
    pgFoot := "any key or tap = back";
    IF pgP < pgPages THEN pgFoot := "any key or tap = next"; END;
    // v3.7a: a caller's footer for the last page (TYPE 2 = a string)
    IF pgP == pgPages AND TYPE(fdFtX) == 2 THEN
      IF DIM(fdFtX) > 0 THEN pgFoot := fdFtX; END;
    END;
    IF pgPages > 1 THEN
      pgFoot := "page " + STRING(pgP,2,0) + " of " + STRING(pgPages,2,0) + ", " + pgFoot;
    END;
    // v3.9: under the two-line verdict band (rows 199-226) the footer
    // moves down 4 px, to row 228; on every other page it stays at 224
    pgFy := 224;
    IF vp >= 0 THEN pgFy := 228; END;
    TEXTOUT_P(pgFoot, G1, 6, pgFy, 1, #1E90FFh, 308);
    BLIT_P(G0, 0, 0, G1);
    fdWaitAny();
  END;
  fdFtX := "";                                   // v3.7a: used once
END;

// The page viewer without a verdict band (every page that is not
// a result: setup, introductions, report notes, About).
fdPage(ttl, lst)
BEGIN
  fdPageV(ttl, lst, -1);
END;

// A dotted vertical line in G1 (LINE_P has no dash style).
fdDotV(dvX, dvY1, dvY2, dvCol)
BEGIN
  LOCAL dvY;
  FOR dvY FROM dvY1 TO dvY2 STEP 3 DO
    PIXON_P(G1, dvX, dvY, dvCol);
  END;
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// Fixed decimals that can never turn scientific (v3.8).  STRING(x,
// 2, d) prints the scientific form whenever 0 < |x| < 10^-d (Lessons
// Learned section 3); rounding first gives 0 or at least 10^-d.
// fdF2 gives two decimals (the papers' precision), fdF1 one.
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdF2(fvX)
BEGIN
  RETURN STRING(ROUND(fvX, 2), 2, 2);
END;

fdF1(fvX)
BEGIN
  RETURN STRING(ROUND(fvX, 1), 2, 1);
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// The upper end of an age interval (v3.9, second review).  FDAES's
// ages stop at 110, so an end above 110 prints "> 110": never a
// computed "110.0", which could put an interval's end at or below its
// own estimate.  Below 110 it is fdF1.  (Lower ends stay MAX(0, ...):
// every estimate with an interval is above 0.)
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdUpEnd(ueX)
BEGIN
  IF ueX > 110 THEN RETURN "> 110"; END;
  RETURN fdF1(ueX);
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// LAMENDIN'S SPREAD -- ONE NAMED CONSTANT (v3.9; Roger's decision D4,
// 19 Oct 2026, 12:42: "the conservative value, 12.5 yr").
// Lamendin et al. (1992) report a MEAN ERROR (ME), "the average
// difference between actual and estimated age" (Table 2's caption,
// p 1377): 10 years on the 306 teeth of the working sample (Table 1,
// p 1376) and 8.4 years on 45 teeth of a forensic control sample
// (Table 2, p 1377).  Whether the signs were ignored is not stated;
// FDAES treats it as the average size of a miss (a mean ABSOLUTE
// difference), as Prince & Ubelaker's "mean difference" is (their Figs
// 5-7 plot the absolute difference).  It is not a standard deviation.
// For normal errors with SD s the mean absolute
// error is s x sqrt(2/pi), so SD = ME x sqrt(pi/2) = 1.2533 x ME:
// 10 x 1.2533 = 12.53, used as 12.5.  Prince & Ubelaker's own four
// groups bear the ratio out: their root mean square error divided by
// their mean absolute difference is 1.30, 1.28, 1.29 and 1.32 (Tables
// 3-6 and 7; fdaes39/PU_BR_tables_v1_NOTES.md).  v3.8a used 10.0, the
// mean error itself, as the SD, which made Lamendin look about 25 %
// more precise than his paper shows.  12.5 is the SD of every
// Lamendin 68 % / 95 % line, P(age >= 18), verdict band and pool
// entry.  This routine is the ONE place the number lives: changing it
// is a one-line change of the RETURN below, and fdLamLbl writes the
// right words for any value.
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdLamSD()
BEGIN
  RETURN 12.5;
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// The words that go with Lamendin's spread on a page (v3.9): three
// lines that say what the number is.  With 12.5 (D4): the paper's mean
// error turned into an SD.  With any other value they say it is
// FDAES's choice and give the paper's figure.
//   llV  the spread in use (fdLamSD())
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdLamLbl(llV)
BEGIN
  IF llV == 12.5 THEN
    RETURN {"Lamendin 1992: mean error 10 yr",
            "(Table 1); as SD of normal errors",
            "10" + " " + CHAR(215) + (" " + CHAR(8730) + "(" + CHAR(960) + "/2) = 12.5 yr")};
  END;
  RETURN {"SD " + fdF2(llV) + " yr: FDAES's choice;",
          "Lamendin's mean error is 10 yr",
          "(Table 1, 306 teeth)."};
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// JOHANSON'S SPREAD FOR ONE TOOTH -- ONE NAMED CONSTANT (v3.9a;
// Roger's decision D5, 19 Oct 2026: 6.0, Johanson's own figure).
// Johanson (1971) Table 8 (p 104; his page numbers are inferred from
// Roger's scan) prints an "Error" by the number of teeth: 6.0 for one
// tooth, 5.2 for two, 4.9, 4.7, then 4.6 down to 4.4 for ten.  The
// table does not say what kind of error; Johanson calls his published
// error figures standard deviations (pp 87-88), and its 5.2 for two
// teeth sits beside his fit's SD of 5.16, so it is read as an SD.
// An independent test (Lucy D, Pollard AM, Roberts CA 1995, J
// Archaeol Sci 22:417-428; manuscript pp 5-6): 35 root sections from
// 24 teeth of 17 people, each root aged alone, missed by 4.5 yr on
// average (a mean absolute deviation: SD about 4.5 x sqrt(pi/2) = 5.6
// for normal errors), and about 68 % fell within +/- 5.16.  v3.9 used
// 8.00, FDAES's own allowance, which no paper gives.  2+ teeth stays
// 5.16, the fit's SD over 46 people, and is NOT made smaller for more
// teeth (as Table 8 would): Lucy et al. 1995 (ms p 8) found that a
// standard error shrunk for combined roots held only 53 % of their
// cases, "the predicted error is underestimated".
// This routine is the ONE place the number lives: the teeth menu, the
// result page (its 68 % / 95 % lines, P(age >= 18) and band), the
// pool, help topic 7 and About take it from here.  The words beside
// it name Table 8 and Lucy 1995: change them too if the value changes.
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdJoh1SD()
BEGIN
  RETURN 6.0;
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// PRINCE & UBELAKER (2002): the four published equations (v3.9,
// Roger's decision D2), set once at INITIALISE.  Typed from the
// reconciled transcription fdaes39/PU_tables_v1.csv (two independent
// readings of the page images, every cell agreeing; see the header,
// PROVENANCE CONFIRMED (v3.9)).  Group g, in the order of the paper's
// Tables 3 to 6 (table number = g + 2):
//   1  black males (Table 3, p 112; text p 111): n = 100, RMSE 8.115402
//   2  white females (Table 4, p 113; text p 112): n = 100, RMSE 10.41143
//   3  black females (Table 5, p 114; text p 112): n = 100, RMSE 11.89553
//   4  white males (Table 6, p 115; text p 112): n = 99, RMSE 9.576177
// fdPUc holds, four to a group, the coefficients of RH, P and T and
// the intercept AS PRINTED IN THE TEXT (pp 111-112):
//   1  A = 1.04 RH + 0.31 P + 0.47 T + 1.70
//   2  A = 1.10 RH + 0.31 P + 0.39 T + 11.82
//   3  A = 1.63 RH + 0.48 P + 0.48 T - 8.41
//   4  A = 0.15 RH + 0.29 P + 0.39 T + 23.17
// The text rounds each Table's estimates, two of them not to the
// nearest: black males' RH is 1.0488111 in Table 3 but 1.04 in the
// text, white males' 0.1587159 in Table 6 but 0.15.  The Tables print
//   1  RH 1.0488111, P 0.3054263, T 0.4662727, intercept 1.7013392
//   2  RH 1.0998269, P 0.3133784, T 0.3875059, intercept 11.816207
//   3  RH 1.6290258, P 0.4801214, T 0.4839498, intercept -8.407472
//   4  RH 0.1587159, P 0.2918081, T 0.3850359, intercept 23.167865
// FDAES uses the printed equations: they are what the authors give
// the reader to use.  At each Table's "Prediction Profile" means the
// printed equations differ from the Tables' fits by -0.19 to +0.17 yr
// (verify_FDAES_v3.9a.py shows each).
// fdPUr: each Table's Root Mean Square Error, the residual SD of the
// fit: the spread FDAES uses.  (The text's "mean difference" and its
// "standard deviation" are of the ABSOLUTE differences: not the SD of
// the errors.)  fdPUn: the number of teeth in each fit.
// No comment may sit inside a list's braces (a Syntax Error on the
// Prime), so every note is here, above the lists.
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdPUTab()
BEGIN
  fdPUc := {1.04, 0.31, 0.47, 1.70,
            1.10, 0.31, 0.39, 11.82,
            1.63, 0.48, 0.48, -8.41,
            0.15, 0.29, 0.39, 23.17};
  fdPUr := {8.115402, 10.41143, 11.89553, 9.576177};
  fdPUn := {100, 100, 100, 99};
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// BANG & RAMM (1970): Tables VIII, IX, X and XI (v3.9, Roger's
// decision D3), set once at INITIALISE.  Typed from the reconciled
// transcription fdaes39/BR_tables_v1.csv (two independent readings
// of the page images, all 528 cells agreeing; see the header).
//   fdBR8   Table VIII (p 21): intact roots (IT), 2nd degree
//   fdBR9   Table IX  (p 22): longitudinal sections (SS), 2nd degree
//           -- four numbers a row: B0, B1, B2, SA
//   fdBR10  Table X   (p 23): intact roots, 1st degree
//   fdBR11  Table XI  (p 24): sections, 1st degree
//           -- three numbers a row: B0, B1, SA
//   fdBRn   the number of teeth in each row (the same in all four)
// Each list has one line per row of the paper, in the paper's order:
//    1  Upper right central incisor
//    2  Upper left central incisor
//    3  Upper right lateral incisor
//    4  Upper left lateral incisor
//    5  Upper right cuspid
//    6  Upper left cuspid
//    7  Upper first premolars
//    8  Upper right second premol.
//    9  Upper left second premol.
//   10  Lower right central incisor
//   11  Lower left central incisor
//   12  Lower right lateral incisor
//   13  Lower left lateral incisor
//   14  Lower right cuspid
//   15  Lower left cuspid
//   16  Lower right first premol.
//   17  Lower left first premol.
//   18  Lower right second premol.
//   19  Lower left second premol.
//   20  Upper 6 molars root no. 1
//   21  Lower 6 molars root no. 1
//   22  Upper 6 molars root no. 2
//   23  Lower 6 molars root no. 2
//   24  Upper 6 molars root no. 3
// ("Upper 6 molars" means the SIX upper molars, not the first molar:
// B&R grouped "corresponding roots from the 6 upper molars and
// corresponding roots from the 6 lower molars, separately", p 19;
// the n's -- 105 upper roots no. 3, 47 lower roots no. 2 -- exceed
// the 34 upper and 14 lower first molars of Tables I-II.  Under each
// table: root no. 1 = mesial, no. 2 = distal, no. 3 = palatinal.
// v3.9's first draft called these rows first molars.)
// Values as printed: Table VIII's first five B0 have one decimal
// (20.3 ...); Table IX's upper right second premolar has B2 = 0.000.
// To check row r of a 2nd-degree table by hand: B0, B1, B2, SA are
// entries 4r-3 .. 4r; age A = B0 + B1 X + B2 X^2.  E.g. Table VIII
// row 1 (upper right central incisor) at X = 6 mm: 20.3 + 5.74 x 6
// + 0.000 x 36 = 54.74 yr, SA 10.42.  A 1st-degree row r: B0, B1,
// SA are entries 3r-2 .. 3r; A = B0 + B1 X.  E.g. Table X row 13
// (lower left lateral incisor) at X = 11 mm: 33.65 + 3.53 x 11 =
// 72.48 yr, SA 11.12 (the line of B&R's Fig. 16, p 20).
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdBRTab()
BEGIN

            18.72, 5.79, -0.082, 11.52,
            30.25, 3.23, -0.018, 10.22,

            34.73, 0.67, 0.211, 10.68,

  fdBR9 := {21.02, 6.03, -0.060, 11.43,

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// PRINCE & UBELAKER: which equation (v3.9).
//   puSx  the Setup sex: 0 unknown, 1 male, 2 female
//   puAn  the Setup ancestry: 0 unknown, 1 white, 2 black, 3 other
// Returns the group of fdPUTab (1 black males, 2 white females, 3
// black females, 4 white males), or 0 when P-U has no equation: the
// sex unknown, or an ancestry other than white or black (the Terry
// Collection sample had no other groups).  The mapping is a data list
// indexed by (ancestry - 1) x 2 + sex: white male, white female,
// black male, black female.
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdPUg(puSx, puAn)
BEGIN
  LOCAL pgL;
  IF puSx < 1 OR puSx > 2 OR puAn < 1 OR puAn > 2 THEN RETURN 0; END;
  pgL := {4, 2, 1, 3};
  RETURN pgL((puAn - 1)*2 + puSx);
END;

// The group's name, for the pages (fdPUTab's order)
fdPUnm(nmG)
BEGIN
  LOCAL nmL;
  nmL := {"black males", "white females", "black females", "white males"};
  RETURN nmL(nmG);
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// PRINCE & UBELAKER's age for group paG, by the equation printed in
// the text: A = c1 RH + c2 P + c3 T + c4, RH the root height in mm,
// P and T the periodontosis and translucency heights x 100 / RH.
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdPUAge(paG, paRH, paP, paT)
BEGIN
  LOCAL paK;
  paK := (paG - 1)*4;
  RETURN fdPUc(paK + 1)*paRH + fdPUc(paK + 2)*paP + fdPUc(paK + 3)*paT + fdPUc(paK + 4);
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// BANG & RAMM, one root (v3.9): its age and SA (B&R p 20).
//   baC  1 intact root (IT), 2 longitudinal section (SS)
//   baR  the row, 1..24 (fdBRTab's order)
//   baX  X = TM in mm: the mean of the shortest and longest extent of
//        transparency from the apex (p 4)
// X <= 9.0 mm: Equation 1, A = B0 + B1 X + B2 X^2, from Table VIII
// (intact) or IX (section).  X > 9.0 mm: Equation 2, A = B0 + B1 X,
// from Table X or XI.  Exactly 9.0 takes Equation 1 ("less than or
// equal to 9.0 mm").  Returns {A, SA, B0, B1, B2, table}, the table
// 8, 9, 10 or 11 (VIII to XI), B2 = 0 for a 1st-degree row.
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdBRAge(baC, baR, baX)
BEGIN
  LOCAL baK, baB0, baB1, baB2, baS, baT;
  IF baX <= 9 THEN
    baK := (baR - 1)*4;
    IF baC == 1 THEN
      baB0 := fdBR8(baK + 1); baB1 := fdBR8(baK + 2);
      baB2 := fdBR8(baK + 3); baS := fdBR8(baK + 4); baT := 8;
    ELSE
      baB0 := fdBR9(baK + 1); baB1 := fdBR9(baK + 2);
      baB2 := fdBR9(baK + 3); baS := fdBR9(baK + 4); baT := 9;
    END;
    RETURN {baB0 + baB1*baX + baB2*baX^2, baS, baB0, baB1, baB2, baT};
  END;
  baK := (baR - 1)*3;
  IF baC == 1 THEN
    baB0 := fdBR10(baK + 1); baB1 := fdBR10(baK + 2); baS := fdBR10(baK + 3); baT := 10;
  ELSE
    baB0 := fdBR11(baK + 1); baB1 := fdBR11(baK + 2); baS := fdBR11(baK + 3); baT := 11;
  END;
  RETURN {baB0 + baB1*baX, baS, baB0, baB1, 0, baT};
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// BANG & RAMM, several roots of ONE person (p 21): the age is the
// mean of the root ages, and its SD is
//   S_A = (1/n) sqrt(Sa1^2 + ... + San^2 + 85 n(n - 1)).
// The 85 is B&R's allowance for the roots of one person moving
// together (p 11): the correlations they could compute between teeth
// of one person (not molars: "this could not be done for molars") ran
// 0.80-0.90, so they put 0.85 x 10 x 10 (a typical SA of 10; "excluding
// molars, S_A values do not differ much") for every pair.  Without it
// the SD would shrink as if the roots were independent; with it two
// roots of SA 10 give 9.62, not 7.07.
//   bcA, bcS  the root ages and their SAs (lists of the same size)
// Returns {A, S_A, the sum of the Sa^2}.
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdBRCmb(bcA, bcS)
BEGIN
  LOCAL bcN, bcI, bcM, bcV;
  bcN := SIZE(bcA);
  bcM := 0; bcV := 0;
  FOR bcI FROM 1 TO bcN DO
    bcM := bcM + bcA(bcI);
    bcV := bcV + bcS(bcI)^2;
  END;
  RETURN {bcM/bcN, (bcV + 85*bcN*(bcN - 1))^0.5 / bcN, bcV};
END;

// A Bang & Ramm table's Roman number: 8..11 -> "VIII" .. "XI"
fdBRrm(rmT)
BEGIN
  LOCAL rmL;
  rmL := {"VIII", "IX", "X", "XI"};
  RETURN rmL(rmT - 7);
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// The help menu (v3.9): the main menu's "How to use / assumptions".
// A topic, then back to this menu; Esc or Cancel returns to the main
// menu.  The Prime numbers the items itself (the tenth is "A").
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdHelpMn()
BEGIN
  LOCAL hmCh, hmGo;
  hmGo := 1;
  WHILE hmGo DO
    hmCh := 0;                    // CANCEL must be distinguishable
    CHOOSE(hmCh, "How to use / assumptions",
      "Using FDAES",
      "Willems 2001",
      "Liversidge 2006",
      "M3 (Mincer 1993)",
      "Lamendin 1992",
      "Prince-Ubelaker 2002",
      "Johanson 1971",
      "Bang & Ramm 1970",
      "Assumptions",
      "What FDAES is not"
    );
    IF hmCh == 0 THEN hmGo := 0; ELSE fdHelp(hmCh); END;
  END;
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// THE PUBLISHED TABLES (v3.8), typed from page images of the papers
// (header: PROVENANCE CONFIRMED (v3.8)).  Called once, at INITIALISE.
// Each table is ONE flat list, written as seven lines of eight
// numbers: line 1 is tooth 31, line 7 tooth 37; the columns are the
// stages A B C D E F G H.  Tooth ti (1..7) at stage st (1..8) is
// entry (ti - 1)*8 + st.  The teeth, in the papers' order:
//   31 central incisor   32 lateral incisor   33 canine
//   34 first premolar (bicuspid)   35 second premolar
//   36 first molar       37 second molar
// No comments may sit INSIDE a list's braces (a Syntax Error on the
// Prime), so every note is here, above the lists.
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdTabs()
BEGIN
  // WILLEMS et al. (2001) Table 1, boys, and Table 2, girls (p 894):
  // "age scores expressed directly in years".  The SUM of the seven
  // teeth's scores is the dental age; one score alone is not an age.
  // -99 marks a cell the paper printed as "..." (no score for that
  // tooth at that stage).  Values exactly as printed (1.5, 0.4, 2),
  // negative ones included: girls' first premolar at A (-0.95) and
  // B (-0.15), second premolar at A (-0.19).  The PDF's text layer
  // drops those three minus signs; the page image has them.
  fdWb := {-99, -99, 1.68, 1.49, 1.5, 1.86, 2.07, 2.19,
           -99, -99, 0.55, 0.63, 0.74, 1.08, 1.32, 1.64,
           -99, -99, -99, 0.04, 0.31, 0.47, 1.09, 1.9,
           0.15, 0.56, 0.75, 1.11, 1.48, 2.03, 2.43, 2.83,
           0.08, 0.05, 0.12, 0.27, 0.33, 0.45, 0.4, 1.15,
           -99, -99, -99, 0.69, 1.14, 1.6, 1.95, 2.15,
           0.18, 0.48, 0.71, 0.8, 1.31, 2, 2.48, 4.17};
  fdWg := {-99, -99, 1.83, 2.19, 2.34, 2.82, 3.19, 3.14,
           -99, -99, -99, 0.29, 0.32, 0.49, 0.79, 0.7,
           -99, -99, 0.6, 0.54, 0.62, 1.08, 1.72, 2,
           -0.95, -0.15, 0.16, 0.41, 0.6, 1.27, 1.58, 2.19,
           -0.19, 0.01, 0.27, 0.17, 0.35, 0.35, 0.55, 1.51,
           -99, -99, -99, 0.62, 0.9, 1.56, 1.82, 2.21,
           0.14, 0.11, 0.21, 0.32, 0.66, 1.28, 2.09, 4.04};
  // LIVERSIDGE et al. (2006) Table X (p 467): "Mean ages of children
  // 'in a tooth formation stage'" -- for each tooth and stage, the
  // mean age in years of the children found IN that stage, and the
  // SD of their ages.  0 marks a stage the table does not give: the
  // early stages no child of the sample (aged 2 to 16.99) was in
  // often enough, and H for every tooth ("it is not possible to
  // predict how much time has passed since the child entered this
  // stage").  Boys' means, then boys' SDs:
  fdLbM := {0, 0, 0, 4.21, 5.38, 6.61, 8.00, 0,
            0, 0, 3.82, 4.68, 6.03, 7.49, 8.83, 0,
            0, 0, 4.54, 5.82, 7.74, 9.78, 12.02, 0,
            0, 3.78, 4.99, 6.64, 8.35, 10.29, 12.14, 0,
            4.32, 4.86, 6.02, 7.46, 9.06, 10.98, 12.94, 0,
            0, 0, 0, 4.02, 5.06, 6.42, 8.66, 0,
            4.07, 4.83, 6.13, 7.96, 9.71, 11.34, 13.42, 0};
  fdLbS := {0, 0, 0, 1.19, 1.00, 0.82, 1.06, 0,
            0, 0, 0.73, 1.08, 1.05, 0.95, 1.10, 0,
            0, 0, 1.19, 1.14, 1.07, 1.22, 1.33, 0,
            0, 0.69, 1.10, 1.04, 1.05, 1.24, 1.23, 0,
            1.37, 1.07, 1.22, 1.16, 1.23, 1.43, 1.42, 0,
            0, 0, 0, 1.40, 1.04, 1.17, 1.40, 0,
            0.55, 0.86, 1.15, 1.13, 1.14, 1.18, 1.43, 0};
  // girls' means, then girls' SDs
  fdLgM := {0, 0, 0, 4.06, 5.14, 6.36, 7.66, 0,
            0, 0, 3.38, 4.42, 5.71, 7.06, 8.31, 0,
            0, 0, 4.15, 5.35, 7.08, 8.81, 10.85, 0,
            0, 3.57, 4.73, 6.26, 7.92, 9.77, 11.46, 0,
            4.14, 4.79, 5.90, 7.29, 8.54, 10.51, 12.33, 0,
            0, 0, 0, 3.74, 4.87, 6.04, 8.22, 0,
            4.05, 4.63, 6.01, 7.67, 9.35, 10.84, 12.92, 0};
  fdLgS := {0, 0, 0, 1.01, 0.74, 0.87, 0.89, 0,
            0, 0, 0.70, 0.96, 0.87, 0.80, 0.98, 0,
            0, 0, 1.02, 1.03, 0.97, 1.08, 1.28, 0,
            0, 1.31, 0.78, 0.96, 0.93, 1.14, 1.18, 0,
            1.43, 0.88, 1.24, 1.09, 1.14, 1.29, 1.32, 0,
            0, 0, 0, 0.77, 0.97, 0.92, 1.28, 0,
            0.67, 0.84, 1.04, 1.05, 1.11, 1.06, 1.41, 0};
  // girls and boys together (used when the Setup sex is unknown):
  // means, then SDs
  fdLcM := {0, 0, 0, 4.15, 5.28, 6.49, 7.85, 0,
            0, 0, 3.64, 4.58, 5.89, 7.30, 8.58, 0,
            0, 0, 4.40, 5.63, 7.46, 9.31, 11.43, 0,
            0, 3.68, 4.88, 6.48, 8.14, 10.04, 11.78, 0,
            4.24, 4.83, 5.97, 7.38, 8.81, 10.74, 12.62, 0,
            0, 0, 0, 3.90, 4.98, 6.25, 8.45, 0,
            4.06, 4.74, 6.08, 7.81, 9.54, 11.08, 13.16, 0};
  fdLcS := {0, 0, 0, 1.12, 0.91, 0.85, 1.00, 0,
            0, 0, 0.75, 1.04, 0.99, 0.91, 1.08, 0,
            0, 0, 1.15, 1.12, 1.08, 1.25, 1.43, 0,
            0, 1.02, 0.99, 1.03, 1.02, 1.22, 1.25, 0,
            1.39, 1.00, 1.23, 1.13, 1.21, 1.38, 1.40, 0,
            0, 0, 0, 1.18, 1.02, 1.09, 1.36, 0,
            0.60, 0.85, 1.10, 1.10, 1.14, 1.15, 1.44, 0};
  // LIVERSIDGE et al. (2006) Table IX (p 465): the mean age of
  // ATTAINING stage H, teeth 31..37 -- boys, then girls.  A tooth at
  // H says only that the child is probably at least about this old.
  fdLhB := {8.34, 9.33, 13.23, 13.04, 14.12, 9.97, 15.25};
  fdLhG := {7.90, 8.81, 11.72, 12.24, 13.47, 9.30, 14.78};
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// Table look-ups (v3.8).  Every routine reads the tables through
// these four, so the odontogram, the result pages and the self-check
// cannot disagree about a number.
//   fdWSc   Willems's score: scSx 1 boys (Table 1), 2 girls (Table
//           2); scT tooth 1..7 (31..37); scS stage 1..8 (A..H).
//           -99 = no score printed.
//   fdLvMn  Liversidge's in-stage mean age, Table X: lmSx 1 boys,
//           2 girls, 0 (sex unknown) girls and boys.  0 = no figure.
//   fdLvSD  the SD that goes with it.
//   fdLvH   Table IX's mean age of attaining H: boys for lhSx 1,
//           otherwise girls -- girls reach H earlier, so with the sex
//           unknown the girls' age keeps "at least" a lower bound.
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdWSc(scSx, scT, scS)
BEGIN
  IF scSx == 2 THEN RETURN fdWg((scT - 1)*8 + scS); END;
  RETURN fdWb((scT - 1)*8 + scS);
END;

fdLvMn(lmSx, lmT, lmS)
BEGIN
  IF lmSx == 1 THEN RETURN fdLbM((lmT - 1)*8 + lmS); END;
  IF lmSx == 2 THEN RETURN fdLgM((lmT - 1)*8 + lmS); END;
  RETURN fdLcM((lmT - 1)*8 + lmS);
END;

fdLvSD(lsSx, lsT, lsS)
BEGIN
  IF lsSx == 1 THEN RETURN fdLbS((lsT - 1)*8 + lsS); END;
  IF lsSx == 2 THEN RETURN fdLgS((lsT - 1)*8 + lsS); END;
  RETURN fdLcS((lsT - 1)*8 + lsS);
END;

fdLvH(lhSx, lhT)
BEGIN
  IF lhSx == 1 THEN RETURN fdLhB(lhT); END;
  RETURN fdLhG(lhT);
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// THE WILLEMS CEILING RULE (v3.9; second review, 19 Oct 2026) -- the
// ONE place it lives.  Willems's tables stop growing near the top:
// all seven teeth at H sum to 16.03 (boys, Table 1) and 15.79 (girls,
// Table 2), and no staging gives more than 16.03 (boys) or 15.93
// (girls, whose Table 2 scores 31 and 32 higher at G than at H: 3.19
// > 3.14 and 0.79 > 0.70).  Willems's children were at most 18.0 years
// old (p 893), so a sum this near the top cannot tell 16 from 30.
// The rule (Roger confirmed, 19 Oct 2026, 15:55): the
// staging is AT THE CEILING when teeth 33 to 37 (columns 3 to 7) are
// all at H, whatever the incisors 31 and 32 show.  Then the page says
// "at least about 15.8-16", draws no band, and nothing goes to the
// pool.  To widen or narrow the rule, change the FOR's first column
// below (1 = only all seven at H; 3 = 33-37, the rule now).
//   wcSt  the seven stage columns (0 = not staged, 1..8 = A..H)
// Returns 1 at the ceiling, else 0.
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdWCeil(wcSt)
BEGIN
  LOCAL wcI;
  FOR wcI FROM 3 TO 7 DO
    IF wcSt(wcI) < 8 THEN RETURN 0; END;
  END;
  RETURN 1;
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// WILLEMS (2001): the sum of the seven scores for ONE sex (v3.8).
//   wuSt  the seven stage columns (0 = not staged, 1..8 = A..H)
//   wuSx  1 boys (Table 1) or 2 girls (Table 2)
// Returns {ok, sum, staged, bad, why, ceiling}:
//   ok 1  every tooth staged, every stage scored: sum = the age
//   ok 0  no age; bad = the first tooth (1..7) that stops it and
//         why = 1 (not staged) or 2 (Willems printed no score)
//   staged = how many of the seven teeth are staged
//   ceiling (v3.9) = fdWCeil: 1 when the staging is at the tables'
//         CEILING (teeth 33-37 all at H; see fdWCeil).  The largest
//         sums any staging gives are 16.03 (boys) and 15.93 (girls),
//         and every dentition past about 16 is there.  Willems's
//         children were at most 18.0 years old, so the sum then says
//         only "at least about 15.8-16": the caller shows no P(age >=
//         18) and offers nothing to the pool (fleet review C-F1;
//         Roger's D7; the second review's near-ceiling rule).
// Willems gives no rule for a missing tooth, so there is no partial
// sum: a score alone is not an age.
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdWSum(wuSt, wuSx)
BEGIN
  LOCAL wuI, wuV, wuSum, wuN, wuBad, wuWhy;
  LOCAL wuH;
  wuSum := 0; wuN := 0; wuBad := 0; wuWhy := 0;
  wuH := fdWCeil(wuSt);           // v3.9: the ceiling rule, one place
  FOR wuI FROM 1 TO 7 DO
    IF wuSt(wuI) == 0 THEN
      IF wuBad == 0 THEN wuBad := wuI; wuWhy := 1; END;
    ELSE
      wuN := wuN + 1;
      wuV := fdWSc(wuSx, wuI, wuSt(wuI));
      IF wuV < -50 THEN
        IF wuBad == 0 THEN wuBad := wuI; wuWhy := 2; END;
      ELSE
        wuSum := wuSum + wuV;
      END;
    END;
  END;
  IF wuBad > 0 THEN RETURN {0, 0, wuN, wuBad, wuWhy, 0}; END;
  RETURN {1, wuSum, wuN, 0, 0, wuH};
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// WILLEMS (2001) for the Setup sex, with its uncertainty (v3.8).
// The uncertainty is the paper's own test of the method on a second
// sample (p 894): dental age minus true age had mean 0.0 and s.d.
// 0.9 yr for boys, mean 0.2 and s.d. 1.3 yr for girls.  The s.d. is
// used as the SE; the mean is stated on the page, not subtracted.
//   weSx 1 boys, 2 girls: the sum from that sex's table, SE 0.9 or
//        1.3.
//   weSx 0 (unknown): BOTH sums, and their 50:50 mixture -- the mean
//        is the average of the two sums, and the SD is
//        sqrt((0.9^2 + 1.3^2)/2 + (difference/2)^2), the spread of a
//        child drawn half the time from each sex's distribution.
// P(age >= 18) for the verdict band.  It is computed as a LOWER tail,
// NORMALD_CDF(m, s, 2m - 18), the same number as 1 - NORMALD_CDF(m,
// s, 18) by symmetry, so that a tiny probability survives in the
// Prime's 12 digits instead of becoming 1 - 1 = 0.  With the sex
// unknown it is the average of the two sexes' probabilities (the
// mixture's own P, not a normal approximation of it).
// Returns {ok, age, SE, P18, boys' sum, girls' sum, staged, bad, why,
// bad sex, all H}; ok 0 leaves the numbers 0 and names the first
// problem (bad, why as fdWSum; bad sex 1 or 2: whose table lacks the
// score).  Element 11 (v3.9) is fdWSum's ceiling flag (fdWCeil: teeth
// 33-37 all at H).  P18 is still computed then, but no page shows it
// and the pool is not offered the age (fdWPage, FDAES()).
// ------------------------------------------------------------
fdWEst(weSt, weSx)
BEGIN
  LOCAL weB, weG, weMn, weSd, weP, weD;
  IF weSx == 1 OR weSx == 2 THEN
    weB := fdWSum(weSt, weSx);
    IF weB(1) == 0 THEN RETURN {0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, weB(3), weB(4), weB(5), weSx, 0}; END;
    weMn := weB(2);
    weSd := 0.9;
    IF weSx == 2 THEN weSd := 1.3; END;
    weP := NORMALD_CDF(weMn, weSd, 2*weMn - 18);
    IF weSx == 1 THEN RETURN {1, weMn, weSd, weP, weMn, 0, 7, 0, 0, 1, weB(6)}; END;
    RETURN {1, weMn, weSd, weP, 0, weMn, 7, 0, 0, 2, weB(6)};
  END;
  // the sex unknown: both tables
  weB := fdWSum(weSt, 1);
  weG := fdWSum(weSt, 2);
  IF weB(1) == 0 THEN RETURN {0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, weB(3), weB(4), weB(5), 1, 0}; END;
  IF weG(1) == 0 THEN RETURN {0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, weG(3), weG(4), weG(5), 2, 0}; END;
  weMn := (weB(2) + weG(2)) / 2;
  weD := (weB(2) - weG(2)) / 2;
  weSd := ((0.9^2 + 1.3^2)/2 + weD^2)^0.5;
  weP := 0.5*NORMALD_CDF(weB(2), 0.9, 2*weB(2) - 18) +
         0.5*NORMALD_CDF(weG(2), 1.3, 2*weG(2) - 18);
  // (the same staging read by both tables: weB(6) = weG(6))
  RETURN {1, weMn, weSd, weP, weB(2), weG(2), 7, 0, 0, 0, weB(6)};
END;

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// LIVERSIDGE (2006), tooth by tooth, combined (v3.8).
//   leSt  the seven stage columns;  leSx  the Setup sex (0, 1, 2)
// Each staged tooth below H with a Table X figure gives a mean age
// m_i and an SD_i.  The estimate is the mean of the k means.  Two
// SEs for it:
//   if the teeth were INDEPENDENT:   sqrt(SD_1^2 + ... + SD_k^2) / k
//   if they MOVE TOGETHER:           (SD_1 + ... + SD_k) / k
// Teeth in one child develop together, so the truth is nearer the
// second, the larger; P(age >= 18) uses it (as a lower tail, see
// fdWEst).  A tooth at H adds no age (Table X has no H) but a floor:
// "at least" Table IX's mean age of attaining H (fdLvH); the largest
// floor is kept.  A tooth at an early stage with no figure is
// counted and left out.
// Returns {k, estimate, SE independent, SE together, P18, teeth at
// H, largest floor, teeth with no figure, teeth staged}; with k = 0
// the four numbers after it are 0.
