        // with the paper's s.d. (or the mixture's SD)
        IF wac == 1 THEN
          means := CONCAT(means, {wr(2)});
          sds   := CONCAT(sds,   {wr(3)});
          lbls  := CONCAT(lbls,  {"Willems 2001"});
          n_est := n_est + 1;
          pok   := 0;
          MSGBOX("Stored. " + STRING(n_est,1,0) + " method(s) active.");
        END;
        // Liversidge: the estimate with the LARGER SE (the teeth move
        // together), the one its verdict band used
        IF wac == 2 THEN
          means := CONCAT(means, {wlr(2)});
          sds   := CONCAT(sds,   {wlr(4)});
          lbls  := CONCAT(lbls,  {"Liversidge 2006"});
          n_est := n_est + 1;
          pok   := 0;
          MSGBOX("Stored. " + STRING(n_est,1,0) + " method(s) active.");
        END;
      ELSE
        MSGBOX("No teeth staged. Nothing stored.");
      END;
    END;
  END;

  // ================================
  // 3  M3 THRESHOLD
  // ================================
  IF ch == 3 THEN
    // Mincer, Harris & Berryman (1993) J Forensic Sci 38(2):379-390,
    // Table 4, as corrected by the journal's erratum (p 1524): the
    // empirical probability (%) of being at least 18, by the GRADE of
    // third-molar formation, by SEX and by JAW, whites only.  The
    // four rows were set at INITIALISE (m3xm, m3xf, m3dm, m3df).
    // NOT an age estimate - threshold probability only; never pooled.
    // v3.7: the Setup sex is applied (unknown shows both sexes; no
    // pooled figure is invented), the jaw is asked, and the verdict
    // is the authors' own three zones, keyed on the grade (p 387).
    m3st := {"D","E","F","G","H"};
    // the jaw first.  Mandible is item 1, so it is the highlighted
    // default; item 3 is the history of the table's one misprint.
    m3j := 0;                   // CANCEL must be distinguishable
    // v3.9: item 4 opens the M3 help topic (its sample, its numbers,
    // the H caveat); like item 3 it returns to the main menu
    CHOOSE(m3j,
      "M3  Which jaw?",
      "Mandible  (lower)",
      "Maxilla  (upper)",
      "Table 4: misprint & erratum",
      "How to read M3 (help)"
    );
    IF m3j == 3 THEN fdM3Hist(m3xm, m3xf, m3dm, m3df); END;
    IF m3j == 4 THEN fdHelp(4); END;
    // then the grade (v3.6's list, without its unsourced percents;
    // v3.7b: Demirjian's wording, the same labels as the odontogram)
    m3c := 0;                   // CANCEL must be distinguishable
    IF m3j == 1 OR m3j == 2 THEN
      CHOOSE(m3c,
        "M3 Stage  [Mincer 1993]",
        "Not visible / absent",
        "D  crown complete to CEJ",
        "E  root shorter than crown",
        ("F  root " + CHAR(8805) + " crown, apex wide"),
        "G  canal walls parallel",
        "H  apex closed"
      );
    END;
    IF m3c >= 2 THEN
      // the chosen jaw's two rows (Mandible unless Maxilla was picked)
      m3jn := "mandible"; m3pm := m3dm; m3pf := m3df;
      IF m3j == 2 THEN m3jn := "maxilla"; m3pm := m3xm; m3pf := m3xf; END;
      // the authors' zones (p 387), keyed on the GRADE, not a cut-point:
      //   1  D (and A-C)  "little likelihood" of being 18
      //   2  E, F, G      "essentially a coin toss (50:50)"
      //   3  H            "reasonably confident" of at least 18
      m3z := 2;
      IF m3c == 2 THEN m3z := 1; END;          // D
      IF m3c == 6 THEN m3z := 3; END;          // H
      // the bars first (both sexes, the chosen jaw), then the page
      fdM3Bars(m3pm, m3pf, m3jn, sex, m3c - 1, m3z);
      // the page: the Setup sex's figure, or both when it is unknown
      IF sex == 0 THEN
        m3L := {"Jaw: " + m3jn + "    Sex: unknown",
                ("P(age " + CHAR(8805) + " 18), males   = ") + fdPct(m3pm(m3c - 1)) + "%",
                ("P(age " + CHAR(8805) + " 18), females = ") + fdPct(m3pf(m3c - 1)) + "%",
                "Sex unknown: both shown, not pooled."};
      ELSE
        IF sex == 1 THEN m3p := m3pm(m3c - 1); m3sx := "male"; END;
        IF sex == 2 THEN m3p := m3pf(m3c - 1); m3sx := "female"; END;
        m3L := {"Jaw: " + m3jn + "    Sex: " + m3sx,
                ("P(age " + CHAR(8805) + " 18) = ") + fdPct(m3p) + "%",
                "P(age  < 18) = " + fdPct(100 - m3p) + "%",
                ""};
      END;
      // the authors' reading, in their words where it fits
      IF m3z == 1 THEN
        m3L := CONCAT(m3L, {"==> Unlikely to be 18.",
                            "The authors: at grades A-D there is",
                            "'little likelihood' of age 18."});
      END;
      IF m3z == 2 THEN
        m3L := CONCAT(m3L, {"==> About a coin toss.",
                            "The authors: grades E, F and G are",
                            "'essentially a coin toss (50:50)'."});
      END;
      IF m3z == 3 THEN
        // the paper's footnote and p 387, in plain words
        m3L := CONCAT(m3L, {("==> Reasonably confident " + CHAR(8805) + " 18."),
                            "CAVEAT: every mature M3 is grade H,",
                            "whatever the age. This figure holds",
                            "only if other evidence shows the",
                            "person is under 25."});
      END;
      m3L := CONCAT(m3L, {"THRESHOLD PROBABILITY ONLY.",
                          "Not an age estimate; not pooled.",
                          "Mincer 1993 Table 4 (erratum), whites"});
      // -1 - m3z = -2, -3 or -4: fdPageV draws the zone band (fdZone)
      fdPageV("M3 THRESHOLD  Stage " + m3st(m3c - 1) + ", " + m3jn, m3L, -1 - m3z);
    END;
  END;

  // ================================
  // 4  LAMENDIN 1992 / PRINCE-UBELAKER 2002
  // ================================
  IF ch == 4 THEN
    // v3.9 (Roger's D2): LAMENDIN 1992 and PRINCE & UBELAKER 2002, from
    // ONE tooth.  Both papers take three heights in mm on the LABIAL
    // surface (Lamendin pp 1374-1375; P-U p 108): the root height RH
    // (CEJ to apex), the periodontosis height (CEJ to the soft-tissue
    // line) and the translucency height (from the apex).  Both use
    // P = periodontosis x 100 / RH and T = translucency x 100 / RH.
    //   Lamendin 1992 (p 1375), always: A = 0.18 P + 0.42 T + 25.53;
    //     spread fdLamSD() = 12.5 (Roger's D4: his mean error, 10 yr,
    //     as the SD of normal errors, 10 x sqrt(pi/2)).
    //   Prince & Ubelaker 2002, when Setup gives the sex and white or
    //     black ancestry: the group's equation AS PRINTED in the text
    //     (fdPUTab, fdPUAge), with RH in mm as a variable of its own;
    //     spread: the group's Table's root mean square error (fdPUr).
    // The two estimates come from ONE tooth, so they are not
    // independent: the pool is offered one of them, never both.  The
    // band uses P-U's estimate when it applies, Lamendin's otherwise,
    // and the page says which.
    // Self-check (Verify Calculations): a white male, RH 15, periodontosis
    // 3, translucency 6 mm: P = 20, T = 40; Lamendin 45.93 yr; P-U
    // (white males) 46.82 yr.
    lgp := fdPUg(sex, anc);
    // the intro page's P-U lines: which equation, or why there is none
    IF lgp > 0 THEN
      lpl := {"P-U 2002: " + fdPUnm(lgp) + " (Table " + STRING(lgp + 2, 1, 0) + "),",
              "  from Setup's sex and ancestry."};
    ELSE
      lpl := {"P-U 2002: needs sex and white or",
              "  black ancestry (Setup)."};
    END;
    fdPage("LAMENDIN 1992 / PRINCE-UBELAKER 2002", {
      "Measure on the LABIAL surface of an",
      "extracted single-rooted tooth, mm:",
      "  RH   root height, CEJ to apex",
      "  per  periodontosis, CEJ to the",
      "       soft-tissue line",
      "  tr   translucency, from the apex",
      "P = per" + mlt + "100 / RH, T = tr" + mlt + "100 / RH",
      "Lamendin 1992: always (poor under",
      "  40: his Table 1).",
      lpl(1), lpl(2),
      "Help: main menu > How to use.",
      "Next: the three heights, in mm."});

    // One form, three heights in mm (the list-of-names form: the values
    // are written back in place, and the call returns 0 on CANCEL).  RH
    // starts at 0, which is refused: a root height must be measured,
    // never left at a placeholder.
    lRH := 0; lPh := 0; lTh := 0;
    iok := INPUT({{lRH,[0]}, {lPh,[0]}, {lTh,[0]}}, "Lamendin / Prince-Ubelaker",
                 {"RH mm:", "Perio mm:", "Transl mm:"},
                 {"root height, CEJ to apex (labial)",
                  "periodontosis, CEJ to soft tissue",
                  "translucency, from the apex"},
                 {0, 0, 0});
    // The refusals, each a teaching message; lbad stays "" when the
    // three heights make sense.  (5 to 35 mm: single roots run about
    // 10 to 20 mm; the range refuses only a slip of the finger.)  The
    // typed values print with two decimals (v3.9, second review), so a
    // refused RH of 4.96 never reads as "5.0".
    lbad := "";
    IF iok == 0 THEN lbad := "Cancelled. Nothing stored."; END;
    IF DIM(lbad) == 0 AND lRH <= 0 THEN
      lbad := "Root height RH must be above 0:" + NL +
              "P and T are heights x 100 / RH." + NL + "Nothing stored.";
    END;
    IF DIM(lbad) == 0 AND (lRH < 5 OR lRH > 35) THEN
      lbad := "RH = " + fdF2(lRH) + " mm is not a root height:" + NL +
              "roots run about 10 to 20 mm;" + NL +
              "FDAES takes 5 to 35 mm." + NL + "Nothing stored.";
    END;
    IF DIM(lbad) == 0 AND (lPh < 0 OR lTh < 0) THEN
      lbad := "A height cannot be negative." + NL + "Nothing stored.";
    END;
    IF DIM(lbad) == 0 AND lPh > lRH THEN
      lbad := "Periodontosis (" + fdF2(lPh) + " mm) cannot" + NL +
              "exceed the root height (" + fdF2(lRH) + " mm):" + NL +
              "it is measured on the root." + NL + "Nothing stored.";
    END;
    IF DIM(lbad) == 0 AND lTh > lRH THEN
      lbad := "Translucency (" + fdF2(lTh) + " mm) cannot" + NL +
              "exceed the root height (" + fdF2(lRH) + " mm):" + NL +
              "it is measured on the root." + NL + "Nothing stored.";
    END;

    IF DIM(lbad) > 0 THEN
      MSGBOX(lbad);
    ELSE
      lP := lPh*100/lRH;
      lT := lTh*100/lRH;
      // Lamendin 1992 (p 1375), with D4's spread
      laL := 0.18*lP + 0.42*lT + 25.53;
      lsL := fdLamSD();
      // Prince & Ubelaker 2002, when Setup gives an equation.  Its
      // estimate is always shown.  It is USED (band, pool) only inside
      // P-U's own sample, ages 25 to 99 (p 108); outside it the
      // equation extrapolates (black females: 1.63 RH - 8.41 is below 0
      // at RH = 5 mm with no periodontosis or translucency), and the
      // page says so (v3.9, second review: the first v3.9 draft used
      // 18 to 110, FDAES's own range, not P-U's).
      lok := 0; laP := 0; lsP := 0;
      IF lgp > 0 THEN
        laP := fdPUAge(lgp, lRH, lP, lT);
        lsP := fdPUr(lgp);
        IF laP >= 25 AND laP <= 99 THEN lok := 1; END;
      END;
      // the verdict band: P-U's estimate when usable, else Lamendin's
      // (the lower tail, as everywhere since v3.8a)
      IF lok == 1 THEN
        vpr := NORMALD_CDF(laP, lsP, 2*laP - 18);
      ELSE
        vpr := NORMALD_CDF(laL, lsL, 2*laL - 18);
      END;
      // the page: the measurements; which estimate the band uses (on
      // page 1, under which the band is drawn); P-U (or why not);
      // Lamendin.  RH prints with two decimals, as P and T do, so each
      // equation line reproduces its age by hand.
      lpl := {"RH = " + fdF2(lRH) + " mm, P = " + fdF2(lP) + ", T = " + fdF2(lT)};
      IF lok == 1 THEN
        lpl := CONCAT(lpl, {"Band: from P-U 2002's estimate."});
      ELSE
        lpl := CONCAT(lpl, {"Band: from Lamendin 1992's estimate."});
      END;
      IF lgp > 0 THEN
        // P-U's printed equation with this tooth's numbers; lk points at
        // the group's four coefficients in fdPUc
        lk := (lgp - 1)*4;
        lpl := CONCAT(lpl, {"P-U 2002, " + fdPUnm(lgp) + " (Table " + STRING(lgp + 2, 1, 0) + "):",
                            fdF2(fdPUc(lk + 1)) + mlt + fdF2(lRH) + " + " + fdF2(fdPUc(lk + 2)) + mlt + fdF2(lP),
                            "  + " + fdF2(fdPUc(lk + 3)) + mlt + fdF2(lT) +
                              IFTE(fdPUc(lk + 4) < 0, " - ", " + ") + fdF2(ABS(fdPUc(lk + 4)))});
        // the estimate itself: "< 0" rather than a negative age
        lpe := fdF2(laP);
        IF laP < 0 THEN lpe := "< 0"; END;
        lpl := CONCAT(lpl, {"= " + lpe + " yr (P-U 2002)"});
        IF lok == 1 THEN
          lpl := CONCAT(lpl, {"SEE " + fdF2(lsP) + " (root mean square",
                              "  error, P-U Table " + STRING(lgp + 2, 1, 0) + ")",
                              "68%: [" + fdF1(MAX(0, laP - lsP)) + ", " + fdUpEnd(laP + lsP) + "]",
                              "95%: [" + fdF1(MAX(0, laP - 2*lsP)) + ", " + fdUpEnd(laP + 2*lsP) + "]"});
        ELSE
          lpl := CONCAT(lpl, {"Outside P-U's sample (25-99, p 108):",
                              "  extrapolation; no band, not pooled."});
        END;
      ELSE
        lpl := CONCAT(lpl, {"P-U 2002: needs sex and white or",
                            "  black ancestry (Setup)."});
      END;
      lpl := CONCAT(lpl, {"LAMENDIN 1992:",
                          "0.18" + mlt + fdF2(lP) + " + 0.42" + mlt + fdF2(lT) + " + 25.53",
                          "= " + fdF2(laL) + " yr (Lamendin 1992)"});
      // what the spread is (D4), then the lines it gives
      llb := fdLamLbl(lsL);
      lpl := CONCAT(lpl, llb);
      lpl := CONCAT(lpl, {"68%: [" + fdF1(MAX(0, laL - lsL)) + ", " + fdUpEnd(laL + lsL) + "]",
                          "95%: [" + fdF1(MAX(0, laL - 2*lsL)) + ", " + fdUpEnd(laL + 2*lsL) + "]"});
      IF laL < 40 THEN lpl := CONCAT(lpl, {"==> Lamendin under 40: unreliable."}); END;
      IF lok == 0 THEN
        // v3.9 (second review): Lamendin's estimate is never below its
        // constant 25.53 (P and T are never negative), so with his
        // spread its P(age >= 18) has a floor; computed here from
        // fdLamSD, so it follows D4 (72.7 % with 12.5)
        lpl := CONCAT(lpl, {"Lamendin cannot give below 25.5 yr:",
                            ("P(age " + CHAR(8805) + " 18) never below ") +
                              fdPctN(NORMALD_CDF(25.53, lsL, 2*25.53 - 18)*100) + "%."});
      END;
      IF lgp > 0 THEN
        lpl := CONCAT(lpl, {"(P-U as printed in the text: its",
                            "Table's estimates, rounded.)"});
      END;
      fdPageV("LAMENDIN 1992 / P-U 2002", lpl, vpr);
      // The pool: ONE of the two, since both read this one tooth.  lk:
      // 1 P-U, 2 Lamendin, 0 neither.
      lpk := 0;                 // CANCEL must be distinguishable
      lk := 0;
      IF lok == 1 THEN
        CHOOSE(lpk, "Add to Bayesian pool?",
          "Prince-Ubelaker 2002",
          "Lamendin 1992",
          "Neither"
        );
        IF lpk == 1 OR lpk == 2 THEN lk := lpk; END;
      ELSE
        CHOOSE(lpk, "Add to Bayesian pool?",
          "Lamendin 1992",
          "Neither"
        );
        IF lpk == 1 THEN lk := 2; END;
      END;
      IF lk == 1 THEN
        means := CONCAT(means, {laP});
        sds   := CONCAT(sds,   {lsP});
        lbls  := CONCAT(lbls,  {"P-U 2002"});
      END;
      IF lk == 2 THEN
        means := CONCAT(means, {laL});
        sds   := CONCAT(sds,   {lsL});
        lbls  := CONCAT(lbls,  {"Lamendin"});
      END;
      IF lk > 0 THEN
        n_est := n_est + 1;
        pok   := 0;
        MSGBOX("Stored. " + STRING(n_est,1,0) + " method(s) active.");
      END;
    END;
  END;

  // ================================
  // 7  JOHANSON 1971
  // ================================
  IF ch == 7 THEN
    // Age = 11.02+5.14A+2.30S+4.14P+3.71C+5.57R+8.98T
    // Self-check: A=1 S=1.5 P=0.5 C=0.5 R=0 T=2.5 => 45.985 yr
    // (v3.9: the blank line after the first gives way to the help
    // pointer, so the page still holds 13 lines)
    // v3.9a: Gustafson summed his six scores, each weighted the same:
    // Formula 1, A + P + S + C + R + T = points (Gustafson 1950, p 49);
    // Table 5's weights above differ.  v3.9's "That ignores the actual
    // biology." had no source and is gone.
    fdPage("JOHANSON (1971)  Histological", {
      "Score each variable 0-3 in 0.5 steps.",
      "DIFFERENTIAL WEIGHTS:",
      ("T " + CHAR(215) + " 8.98  Root Transparency [BEST]"),
      ("R " + CHAR(215) + " 5.57  Root Resorption"),
      ("A " + CHAR(215) + " 5.14  Attrition"),
      ("P " + CHAR(215) + " 4.14  Periodontosis"),
      ("C " + CHAR(215) + " 3.71  Cementum Apposition"),
      ("S " + CHAR(215) + " 2.30  Secondary Dentin [LEAST]"),
      "",
      "Gustafson summed all six equally",
      "(1950, p 49); these weights differ.",
      "Help: main menu > How to use.",
      "You will enter 6 scores next."});

    // One form for all six scores. The return is tested, so CANCEL no
    // longer falls through with zeros (all-zero gives 11.02 yr, which the
    // range guard then rejected with a message blaming the user's scores).
    jA := 0; jS := 0; jP := 0; jC := 0; jR := 0; jT := 0;
    iok := INPUT({{jA,[0]}, {jS,[0]}, {jP,[0]}, {jC,[0]}, {jR,[0]}, {jT,[0]}}, "Johanson (1971) scores",
                 {"A attrition:", "S sec.dentin:", "P periodont.:",
                  "C cementum:", "R resorption:", "T transparency:"},
                 {"0 to 3 in 0.5 steps", "0 to 3 in 0.5 steps",
                  "0 to 3 in 0.5 steps", "0 to 3 in 0.5 steps",
                  "0 to 3 in 0.5 steps",
                  "0 to 3 in 0.5 steps  [best predictor]"},
                 {0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0});

    jval := 1;
    IF iok == 0 THEN jval := 0; END;
    jsc  := {jA, jS, jP, jC, jR, jT};
    FOR jvi FROM 1 TO 6 DO
      IF jsc(jvi) < 0 OR jsc(jvi) > 3 THEN jval := 0; END;
      IF FP(jsc(jvi)*2) > 0.001 THEN jval := 0; END;
    END;

    IF jval == 0 THEN
      MSGBOX("Cancelled, or a score was not" + NL +
             "0, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 2.5, or 3." + NL + "Nothing stored.");
    ELSE
      // v3.9 (fleet review C-F5; decision D5 is OPEN): the two spreads
      // keep their values and say what they are.  5.16 is the residual
      // SD of Johanson's six-variable fit ("Standard deviation = 5.16",
      // printed under VI in "Evaluation of the constants"; the fit's
      // coefficients are Table 5's line VI).  The fit's sum runs over
      // i = 1 ... 46, and "the whole material consists of 46
      // individuals" (162 teeth; one to eleven teeth each, "Material"):
      // 46 PEOPLE, not 46 teeth (the review and the brief said teeth):
      // 4 of them gave one tooth, the rest 2 to 11.  With more than one
      // tooth his procedure takes the mean value.
      // v3.9a (Roger's D5, 19 Oct 2026): ONE tooth takes fdJoh1SD(),
      // 6.0, Johanson's own Table 8 figure (v3.9: 8.00, FDAES's own
      // allowance from no paper); see fdJoh1SD for the sources.  The
      // menu is the list form so its first item can carry the number
      // from fdJoh1SD (ChiTable's menus build items the same way); "his
      // Table 8" keeps the item to 27 characters (the house limit, 29).
      jnc := 0;                 // CANCEL must be distinguishable
      CHOOSE(jnc, "Number of teeth averaged?", {
        "1 tooth   " + fdF1(fdJoh1SD()) + " (his Table 8)",
        "2+ teeth  5.16 (Johanson SD)"});
      // v3.5: CANCEL here stores nothing.  v3.4 silently took it as
      // "1 tooth" and went on to compute and offer the estimate.
      IF jnc == 0 THEN
        MSGBOX("Cancelled. Nothing stored.");
      ELSE
        IF jnc == 2 THEN jse := 5.16; ELSE jse := fdJoh1SD(); END;

        // Self-check: 11.02+5.14+3.45+2.07+1.855+0+22.45 = 45.985 yr
        ae := 11.02 + 5.14*jA + 2.30*jS + 4.14*jP +
               3.71*jC + 5.57*jR + 8.98*jT;
        sv := jse;

        IF ae >= 18 AND ae <= 110 THEN
          // v3.8a: the lower tail, as in Lamendin above
          vpr := NORMALD_CDF(ae, sv, 2*ae - 18); // for the verdict band
          // v3.9 (C-F5): the spread in words, as the menu now names it.
          // 5.16 is the SD of the fit of Table 5, line VI, over 46
          // people: "From 4 of the 46 patients only one tooth was
          // extracted whereas from the rest 2 to 11 teeth were removed"
          // ("Material"), and "the 'mouth' (2 teeth or more) has been
          // the entity instead of single teeth".
          // For ONE tooth (v3.9a, Roger's D5): Johanson's Table 8, 6.0
          // (fdJoh1SD), and what an independent test found: Lucy,
          // Pollard & Roberts 1995 (ms pp 5-6), 17 people, a mean
          // absolute deviation of 4.5 yr, about 5.6 as an SD.  (v3.9:
          // "SD 8.00 yr = FDAES's allowance;")
          IF jnc == 2 THEN
            jlb := {"SD 5.16 yr = SD of Johanson's fit,",
                    "Table 5 (46 people, 1-11 teeth each)"};
          ELSE
            jlb := {"1 tooth: SD " + fdF1(fdJoh1SD()) + " (Johanson Table 8;",
                    "  Lucy 1995, 17 new people: ~5.6)."};
          END;
          // v3.9 (C-F9): every term spaced, "T = 2.5 x 8.98 = 22.450".
          // Three decimals: a score in 0.5 steps times a two-decimal
          // coefficient is exact to three, so the six terms and the
          // intercept add up to the age exactly.  ROUND first, so a
          // fixed decimal can never turn scientific.  The last two lines
          // (C-F5): Johanson's own running text prints the transparency
          // coefficient as 9.98, his Table 5 as 8.98; FDAES uses Table 5.
          // (The stepwise fits' T coefficients run 13.62, 11.93, 11.41,
          // 11.29, 9.97, which if anything favours 9.98; the evidence for
          // 8.98 is Table 5 itself and his procedure's worked example,
          // 44.92 yr computed with 8.98.)  At T = 2.5 they differ by 2.5 yr.
          fdPageV("JOHANSON (1971) result", {
            "T = " + STRING(jT,2,1) + mlt + "8.98 = " + STRING(ROUND(8.98*jT,3),2,3),
            "R = " + STRING(jR,2,1) + mlt + "5.57 = " + STRING(ROUND(5.57*jR,3),2,3),
            "A = " + STRING(jA,2,1) + mlt + "5.14 = " + STRING(ROUND(5.14*jA,3),2,3),
            "P = " + STRING(jP,2,1) + mlt + "4.14 = " + STRING(ROUND(4.14*jP,3),2,3),
            "C = " + STRING(jC,2,1) + mlt + "3.71 = " + STRING(ROUND(3.71*jC,3),2,3),
            "S = " + STRING(jS,2,1) + mlt + "2.30 = " + STRING(ROUND(2.30*jS,3),2,3),
            "Intercept = 11.020",
            "Age = " + STRING(ROUND(ae,3),2,3) + " yr",
            jlb(1), jlb(2),
            "68%: [" + fdF1(MAX(0,ae-sv)) +
              ", " + fdUpEnd(ae+sv) + "]",
            "95%: [" + fdF1(MAX(0,ae-2*sv)) +
              ", " + fdUpEnd(ae+2*sv) + "]",
            "Johanson's text prints 9.98T, his",
            "Table 5 8.98T; FDAES uses Table 5."}, vpr);
          ch_yn := 0;           // CANCEL must be distinguishable
          CHOOSE(ch_yn, "Add to Bayesian pool?", "Yes", "No");
          IF ch_yn == 1 THEN
            means := CONCAT(means, {ae});
            sds   := CONCAT(sds,   {sv});
            lbls  := CONCAT(lbls,  {"Johanson"});
            n_est := n_est + 1;
            pok   := 0;
            MSGBOX("Stored. " + STRING(n_est,1,0) + " method(s) active.");
          END;
        ELSE
          MSGBOX("Age " + STRING(ae,2,1) + " yr outside" + NL +
                 "valid range 18-110. Check scores.");
        END;
      END;
    END;
  END;

  // ================================
  // 8  BANG & RAMM 1970
  // ================================
  IF ch == 8 THEN
    // v3.9 (Roger's D3): BANG & RAMM'S OWN METHOD (B&R 1970, pp 20-24).
    // The variable X is TM (p 4) of ONE root: the mean of the shortest
    // (TL1) and the longest (TL2) extent of transparent dentin, each
    // measured from the apex (Fig. 1: TL1 = 6.0 and TL2 = 9.0 mm give
    // TM = 7.5 mm), on the intact root (IT) or a longitudinal section
    // (SS); multi-rooted teeth are measured root by root (p 4).  (The
    // first v3.9 draft called X a length "on the buccal midline": that
    // is how B&R measured the ROOT length, not the transparency.)
    //   X <= 9.0 mm: Equation 1, A = B0 + B1 X + B2 X^2, Table VIII
    //     (intact) or IX (section);
    //   X > 9.0 mm: Equation 2, A = B0 + B1 X, Table X or XI
    //     ("usually will give the best results", p 20);
    // coefficients and SA per tooth AND side.  The MOLAR rows are not
    // first molars: B&R grouped "corresponding roots from the 6 upper
    // molars and corresponding roots from the 6 lower molars,
    // separately" (p 19), so a row "Upper 6 molars root no. 1" serves
    // root no. 1 of any of the six upper molars.  The rows are in
    // fdBRTab (with how a student checks one by hand); the arithmetic
    // is fdBRAge.  Several roots of ONE person: each root aged alone,
    // then A = their mean and S_A = (1/n) sqrt(Sa1^2 + ... + San^2 +
    // 85 n(n - 1)) (p 21; fdBRCmb).  Only that one combined value is
    // offered to the pool: two roots of one person are not two
    // independent estimates.  B&R's own advice for several teeth (p 19):
    // the best estimate comes "by excluding upper first premolars and
    // all the molars"; the one-person page repeats it when such a root
    // is in the list.  v3.8a's line, 14.8 + 1.35 T% for four teeth, was
    // not in the paper and is gone.
    // Refused (v3.9, second review), each with a message, the person
    // then finished with the roots already measured: X below 0 or above
    // 25 mm; the same tooth, side and root twice for one person (intact
    // and section of one root are ONE root); an age above 110 yr, which
    // no table supports.  Above 14 mm (the end of B&R's figures) the page
    // warns.
    // THE MENUS AS DATA: the tooth menu's twelve items (brNm), then a
    // second menu that depends on the tooth (brKd: 0 none, 1 Right /
    // Left, 2 the molar's root), and the table row for its 1st, 2nd and
    // 3rd answer (brR1, brR2, brR3; rows in fdBRTab's order).  Upper
    // first premolars have one row for both sides, as the tables print.
    brNm := {"Upper central incisor", "Upper lateral incisor",
             "Upper canine", "Upper first premolar",
             "Upper second premolar", "Upper molar (any of six)",
             "Lower central incisor", "Lower lateral incisor",
             "Lower canine", "Lower first premolar",
             "Lower second premolar", "Lower molar (any of six)"};
    brKd := {1, 1, 1, 0, 1, 2, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 2};
    brR1 := {1, 3, 5, 7, 8, 20, 10, 12, 14, 16, 18, 21};
    brR2 := {2, 4, 6, 7, 9, 22, 11, 13, 15, 17, 19, 23};
    brR3 := {0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 24, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0};
    fdPage("BANG & RAMM (1970)", {
      "X = TM (mm) of ONE root: the mean of",
      "the shortest and longest extent of",
      "transparency from the apex, on the",
      "intact root or a longitudinal",
      "section (B&R p 4).",
      ("X " + CHAR(8804) + " 9.0 mm: the 2nd-degree"),
      "  equation (Table VIII or IX);",
      "X > 9.0 mm: the 1st-degree",
      "  equation (Table X or XI).",
      "Several roots of one person are",
      "  combined into ONE age (p 21).",
      "Help: main menu > How to use.",
      "Next: the root, the tooth, X."});
    // this person's roots so far: ages, SAs, page lines, table rows;
    // brFl becomes 1 when a molar root or an upper first premolar is
    // among them (B&R p 19's advice, on the one-person page)
    brAg := {}; brSs := {}; brLn := {}; brRs := {};
    brFl := 0;
    brgo := 1;
    WHILE brgo DO
      brOk := 0;
      brc := 0;                 // CANCEL must be distinguishable
      CHOOSE(brc, "Bang & Ramm: the root",
        "Intact root (IT)",
        "Longitudinal section (SS)"
      );
      brt := 0;                 // CANCEL must be distinguishable
      IF brc > 0 THEN
        CHOOSE(brt, "Bang & Ramm: which tooth?",
          "Upper central incisor", "Upper lateral incisor",
          "Upper canine", "Upper first premolar",
          "Upper second premolar", "Upper molar (any of six)",
          "Lower central incisor", "Lower lateral incisor",
          "Lower canine", "Lower first premolar",
          "Lower second premolar", "Lower molar (any of six)"
        );
      END;
      brs := 0;                 // CANCEL must be distinguishable
      IF brt > 0 THEN
        // the second menu, as brKd says: none, the side, or the root
        IF brKd(brt) == 0 THEN brs := 1; END;
        IF brKd(brt) == 1 THEN
          brs := 0;             // CANCEL must be distinguishable
          CHOOSE(brs, brNm(brt), "Right", "Left");
        END;
        IF brKd(brt) == 2 AND brt == 6 THEN
          brs := 0;             // CANCEL must be distinguishable
          CHOOSE(brs, brNm(brt), "Mesial root (no. 1)", "Distal root (no. 2)", "Palatal root (no. 3)");
        END;
        IF brKd(brt) == 2 AND brt == 12 THEN
          brs := 0;             // CANCEL must be distinguishable
          CHOOSE(brs, brNm(brt), "Mesial root (no. 1)", "Distal root (no. 2)");
        END;
      END;
      IF brs > 0 THEN
        // the row, from the data lists; and the root's name for the pages
        brRw := brR1(brt);
        IF brs == 2 THEN brRw := brR2(brt); END;
        IF brs == 3 THEN brRw := brR3(brt); END;
        brWh := brNm(brt);
        IF brKd(brt) == 0 THEN brWh := brWh + " (both)"; END;
        IF brKd(brt) == 1 THEN brWh := brWh + IFTE(brs == 1, ", right", ", left"); END;
        IF brKd(brt) == 2 THEN
          // a molar: B&R's row serves any of the six molars of that jaw
          brWh := IFTE(brt == 6, "Any upper molar", "Any lower molar");
          brL := {", mesial root", ", distal root", ", palatal root"};
          brWh := brWh + brL(brs);
        END;
        // the same root twice for one person?  (intact and section of
        // one root are one root: the row does not depend on brc)
        brDp := 0;
        FOR brI FROM 1 TO SIZE(brRs) DO
          IF brRs(brI) == brRw THEN brDp := 1; END;
        END;
        IF brDp == 1 THEN
          MSGBOX("This root is already in this" + NL +
                 "person's list (intact or section):" + NL +
                 "one value per root." + NL + "This root not used.");
        ELSE
          // X in mm, 0 to 25: a length cannot be negative, and no root
          // is 25 mm long (single roots run about 10 to 20 mm; B&R's
          // Fig. 16 runs to 14 mm), so the limit refuses only a slip of
          // the finger.  The typed value prints with two decimals.
          brX := 0;
          iok := INPUT({{brX,[0]}}, "Bang & Ramm (1970)",
                       {"X mm:"},
                       {"TM = mean of shortest and longest extent"},
                       {0});
          IF iok == 0 THEN
            MSGBOX("Cancelled: this root not used.");
          ELSE
            IF brX < 0 THEN
              MSGBOX("The transparent length X cannot" + NL +
                     "be negative: it is a length." + NL + "This root not used.");
            ELSE
              IF brX > 25 THEN
                MSGBOX("X = " + fdF2(brX) + " mm: longer than any" + NL +
                       "root (FDAES takes 0 to 25 mm)." + NL + "This root not used.");
              ELSE
                // the age and SA of this root (fdBRAge); above 110 yr
                // no table supports it (B&R's oldest were 80+)
                brV := fdBRAge(brc, brRw, brX);
                IF brV(1) > 110 THEN
                  MSGBOX("A = " + fdF2(brV(1)) + " yr: above 110," + NL +
                         "beyond any age the tables were" + NL +
                         "built on (B&R's oldest: 80+)." + NL + "This root not used.");
                ELSE
                  brOk := 1;
                END;
              END;
            END;
          END;
        END;
      END;
      IF brOk == 1 THEN
        // this root's page
        brL := {brWh,
                IFTE(brc == 1, "Intact: Table ", "Section: Table ") + fdBRrm(brV(6)) +
                  " (p " + STRING(brV(6) + 13, 1, 0) + "), n = " + STRING(fdBRn(brRw), 1, 0)};
        IF brV(6) <= 9 THEN
          brL := CONCAT(brL, {"X = " + fdF2(brX) + (" mm, " + CHAR(8804) + " 9.0: 2nd degree"),
                              ("A = B0 + B1 X + B2 X" + CHAR(178)),
                              "B0 = " + fdF2(brV(3)) + ", B1 = " + fdF2(brV(4)) + ", B2 = " +
                                STRING(ROUND(brV(5), 3), 2, 3)});
        ELSE
          brL := CONCAT(brL, {"X = " + fdF2(brX) + " mm, > 9.0: 1st degree",
                              "A = B0 + B1 X",
                              "B0 = " + fdF2(brV(3)) + ", B1 = " + fdF2(brV(4))});
        END;
        // the intervals: +/- SA and +/- 2 SA are 68 % and 95 % of a
        // normal curve; B&R's Table XII calls them ca. 65 % and ca. 95 %
        // and adds +/- 0.45 SA, ca. 35 % (p 24).  An upper end above 110
        // prints "> 110" (fdUpEnd).
        brL := CONCAT(brL, {"= " + fdF2(brV(1)) + " yr",
                            "SA = " + fdF2(brV(2)) + " yr (that row of the table)",
                            "68%: [" + fdF1(MAX(0, brV(1) - brV(2))) + ", " + fdUpEnd(brV(1) + brV(2)) + "] (normal;",
                            "  B&R Table XII: ca. 65%)",
                            "95%: [" + fdF1(MAX(0, brV(1) - 2*brV(2))) + ", " + fdUpEnd(brV(1) + 2*brV(2)) + "] (B&R: ca. 95%)",
                            "B&R Table XII (p 24), ca. 35%:",
                            ("  A " + CHAR(177) + " 0.45 SA = [") + fdF1(MAX(0, brV(1) - 0.45*brV(2))) + ", " +
                              fdUpEnd(brV(1) + 0.45*brV(2)) + "]"});
        // near 9.0 mm the method changes equation: say so, with the age
        // the other equation would give (B&R switch at 9.0, p 20)
        IF brX >= 8 AND brX <= 10 THEN
          IF brX <= 9 THEN brQ := fdBRAge(brc, brRw, 9.01); ELSE brQ := fdBRAge(brc, brRw, 9); END;
          brOt := brQ(3) + brQ(4)*brX + brQ(5)*brX^2;
          brL := CONCAT(brL, {"X near 9.0 mm, where B&R switch",
                              "equations: Table " + fdBRrm(brQ(6)) + " gives " + fdF2(brOt) + " yr."});
        END;
        // beyond 14 mm, the end of B&R's figures (Fig. 16)
        IF brX > 14 THEN
          brL := CONCAT(brL, {"X > 14 mm: beyond the range of",
                              "B&R's figures (they end at 14)."});
        END;
        // the band from N(A, SA), the lower tail as everywhere
        vpr := NORMALD_CDF(brV(1), brV(2), 2*brV(1) - 18);
        fdPageV("BANG & RAMM (1970)  root " + STRING(SIZE(brAg) + 1, 1, 0), brL, vpr);
        brAg := CONCAT(brAg, {brV(1)});
        brSs := CONCAT(brSs, {brV(2)});
        brRs := CONCAT(brRs, {brRw});
        IF brKd(brt) == 2 OR brt == 4 THEN brFl := 1; END;
        brLn := CONCAT(brLn, {STRING(SIZE(brAg), 1, 0) + ": " + brWh,
                              "   " + fdF2(brV(1)) + " yr, SA " + fdF2(brV(2)) + " (Table " + fdBRrm(brV(6)) + ")"});
        brMore := 0;            // CANCEL must be distinguishable
        CHOOSE(brMore, "Another root, same person?",
          "No: finish this person",
          "Yes: add another root"
        );
        IF brMore <> 2 THEN brgo := 0; END;
      ELSE
        // a cancelled menu or a refused root ends this person: the roots
        // already measured (if any) are finished below
        brgo := 0;
      END;
    END;

    IF SIZE(brAg) == 0 THEN
      MSGBOX("No root measured. Nothing stored.");
    ELSE
      ae := brAg(1); sv := brSs(1);
      IF SIZE(brAg) > 1 THEN
        // several roots of one person: B&R's mean and S_A (fdBRCmb)
        brCb := fdBRCmb(brAg, brSs);
        ae := brCb(1); sv := brCb(2);
        brL := CONCAT({"One person, " + STRING(SIZE(brAg), 1, 0) + " roots (B&R p 21):"}, brLn);
        brL := CONCAT(brL, {"A = the mean of the " + STRING(SIZE(brAg), 1, 0) + " ages",
                            "= " + fdF2(ae) + " yr",
                            ("S_A = (1/n) " + CHAR(8730) + "(sum Sa" + CHAR(178) + " + 85n(n-1))"),
                            "  = (1/" + STRING(SIZE(brAg), 1, 0) + (") " + CHAR(8730) + "(") + fdF2(brCb(3)) + " + " +
                              STRING(85*SIZE(brAg)*(SIZE(brAg) - 1), 1, 0) + ")",
                            "= " + fdF2(sv) + " yr",
                            "68%: [" + fdF1(MAX(0, ae - sv)) + ", " + fdUpEnd(ae + sv) + "] (normal;",
                            "  B&R Table XII: ca. 65%)",
                            "95%: [" + fdF1(MAX(0, ae - 2*sv)) + ", " + fdUpEnd(ae + 2*sv) + "] (B&R: ca. 95%)",
                            "Table XII, ca. 35%: [" + fdF1(MAX(0, ae - 0.45*sv)) + ", " + fdUpEnd(ae + 0.45*sv) + "]",
                            "(85 = 0.85" + mlt + "10" + mlt + "10: teeth of one",
                            "person (not molars) correlate r",
                            "0.80-0.90, p 11.)"});
        // B&R's advice for several teeth (p 19), when it applies
        IF brFl == 1 THEN
          brL := CONCAT(brL, {"B&R (p 19): with several teeth, the",
                              "best estimate leaves out 'upper",
                              "first premolars and all the molars'."});
        END;
        vpr := NORMALD_CDF(ae, sv, 2*ae - 18);
        fdPageV("BANG & RAMM: one person", brL, vpr);
      END;
      // the pool: ONE value for this person (one root, or the roots
      // combined), never the roots one by one
      ch_yn := 0;               // CANCEL must be distinguishable
      CHOOSE(ch_yn, "Add to Bayesian pool?", "Yes", "No");
      IF ch_yn == 1 THEN
        means := CONCAT(means, {ae});
        sds   := CONCAT(sds,   {sv});
        lbls  := CONCAT(lbls,  {"Bang-Ramm"});
        n_est := n_est + 1;
        pok   := 0;
        MSGBOX("Stored. " + STRING(n_est,1,0) + " method(s) active.");
      END;
    END;
  END;

  // ================================
  // 9  BAYESIAN COMBINATION
  // ================================
  IF ch == 9 THEN
    IF n_est == 0 THEN
      // v3.9 (second review): every method that can be pooled
      MSGBOX("No estimates stored yet." + NL +
             "Poolable: Willems, Liversidge," + NL +
             "Lamendin, P-U, Johanson, Bang &" + NL +
             "Ramm. Run one, then choose it at" + NL +
             "the 'Add to pool' question.");
    ELSE
      // Inverse-variance weighted posterior.
      // w_i = 1/sigma_i^2
      // mu_post = SUM(mu_i*w_i) / SUM(w_i)
      // SD_post = SQRT(1/SUM(w_i)) * 1.25, then floored (below)
      // *1.25: the methods are not independent.  Lamendin, Prince-
      // Ubelaker, Johanson and Bang & Ramm all read root transparency
      // (v3.9: Lamendin & Johanson share P and T, P-U uses Lamendin's
      // P and T, B&R measures the transparent length itself); the
      // pool's labels: "Willems 2001", "Liversidge 2006", "Lamendin",
      // "P-U 2002", "Johanson", "Bang-Ramm".  1.25 is FDAES's own
      // allowance, from no paper (help topic "Assumptions").

      tp := 0;
      ws := 0;
      FOR idx FROM 1 TO n_est DO
        prc := 1 / (sds(idx)^2);
        tp  := tp + prc;
        ws  := ws + means(idx) * prc;
      END;
      pmu := ws / tp;
      psd := SQRT(1/tp) * 1.25;

      // PRECISION FLOOR (added v3.4). Inverse-variance pooling assumes the
      // estimates are INDEPENDENT. They are not: Lamendin and Johanson both
      // score root transparency, on the same tooth, by the same observer,
      // so their errors are positively correlated. Without a floor the
      // documented example produced 5.11 yr from inputs of 10.0 / 5.16 /
      // 9.0 - TIGHTER than the best single method, i.e. the combination
      // manufactured precision no contributing method possesses. The 1.25
      // factor is a fudge, not a measured covariance (1.263 would have been
      // needed on that example alone). Until the between-method covariance
      // is measured, never report a posterior SD smaller than the best
      // contributor. This errs conservative, which is the only acceptable
      // direction for a forensic estimate.
      // pfl (v3.9, second review): 1 when the floor was applied, so
      // the page says so only then
      pfl := 0;
      IF n_est > 1 THEN
        smin := sds(1);
        FOR idx FROM 2 TO n_est DO
          IF sds(idx) < smin THEN smin := sds(idx); END;
        END;
        IF psd < smin THEN psd := smin; pfl := 1; END;
      END;
      pok := 1;

      // v3.9 (second review): the upper ends are not clamped at 110;
      // fdUpEnd prints an end above 110 as "> 110"
      c68lo := MAX(0,   pmu - psd);
      c68hi :=          pmu + psd;
      c95lo := MAX(0,   pmu - 2*psd);
      c95hi :=          pmu + 2*psd;

      // P(age>=t) = 1 - NORMALD_CDF(mu, sd, t), computed as the lower
      // tail NORMALD_CDF(mu, sd, 2mu - t) (v3.8a): the same number by
      // symmetry, but a tail below about 5E-13 is not rounded to 1 - 1 = 0
      // in the Prime's 12 digits.  fdPctN prints them (an exact 0 or 100
      // from the model shows as "< 0.1" / "> 99.9").
      p16 := NORMALD_CDF(pmu, psd, 2*pmu - 16) * 100;
      p18 := NORMALD_CDF(pmu, psd, 2*pmu - 18) * 100;
      p21 := NORMALD_CDF(pmu, psd, 2*pmu - 21) * 100;

      // The page: the numbers first, so they are on page 1 whatever
      // the number of methods; then one line per stored method; then
      // the two method notes, which spill onto a second page only
      // with three or more methods (12 lines above the verdict band;
      // v3.6 dropped the blank line after the CIs so two still fit).
      // wmsg is a LIST of lines here.
      wmsg := {
        "Mean:   " + fdF2(pmu) + " yr",
        "SD:     " + fdF2(psd) + " yr",
        "68% CI: [" + fdF1(c68lo) + ", " + fdUpEnd(c68hi) + "]",
        "95% CI: [" + fdF1(c95lo) + ", " + fdUpEnd(c95hi) + "]",
        ("P(age " + CHAR(8805) + " 16): ") + fdPctN(p16) + "%",
        ("P(age " + CHAR(8805) + " 18): ") + fdPctN(p18) + "%",
        ("P(age " + CHAR(8805) + " 21): ") + fdPctN(p21) + "%",
        "Weights:"};
      FOR widx FROM 1 TO n_est DO
        wprc := 1 / (sds(widx)^2);
        ww   := wprc / tp * 100;
        wmsg := CONCAT(wmsg, {"  " + lbls(widx) + ": " + fdPct(ww) + "%"});
      END;
      // the floor's line says what happened (v3.9, second review: was
      // "SD floored at best single SE" whatever happened)
      wmsg := CONCAT(wmsg, {"Inv-var weighted  *1.25 SD"});
      IF pfl == 1 THEN
        wmsg := CONCAT(wmsg, {"SD raised to best single SD (floor)"});
      ELSE
        IF n_est == 1 THEN
          wmsg := CONCAT(wmsg, {"One method: no floor applies"});
        ELSE
          wmsg := CONCAT(wmsg, {("Floor not needed (SD " + CHAR(8805) + " best SD)")});
        END;
      END;
      fdPageV("BAYESIAN POSTERIOR", wmsg, p18/100);
      // v3.6: the posterior curve.  Its Table soft key shows the
      // threshold table (the v3.5 numbers page) and Forest the forest
      // plot; each returns here, at the same threshold, until Done.
      pthr := 18;
      pact := 1;
      WHILE pact > 0 DO
        pres := fdPost(pmu, psd, pthr);
        pact := pres(1);
        pthr := pres(2);
        IF pact == 1 THEN fdThrTable(pmu, psd); END;
        IF pact == 2 THEN fdForest(means, sds, lbls, pmu, psd); END;
      END;
      // v3.9 (fleet review C-F3): Gustafson's figure as Lamendin et al.
      // (1992, p 1377) report it -- a MEAN ERROR on 39 individuals,
      // Gustafson's method 14.2 +/- 3.4 yr against Lamendin's own
      // 8.9 +/- 2.2 yr -- not "SE".
      // v3.9 (Roger, 19 Oct 2026, 12:48): the page most likely to be
      // photographed or copied into a report says on its first line
      // that FDAES is a teaching aid; when a Lamendin estimate is in the
      // pool, the page also says what its 12.5 is (D4), and as it then
      // runs to a second page, that page ends with the same line.  To
      // keep the usual page at 12 lines the blank line after the numbers
      // is gone and the limitation and Gustafson sentences are shorter.
      // v3.9 (second review): the report carries only checked
      // statements (v3.8a's "(Saunders 1837; Cattell 1928 n=7689)" was
      // not checked against any source and is gone), and its first and
      // last lines are the fleet's disclaimer
      // v3.9a: "Biological variation irreducible." was a claim no
      // source read here makes; the line now says what the SDs are
      wmsg := {"Teaching & demonstration aid;",
        "not for legal use.",
        "Age: " + fdF1(pmu) + " yr",
        "95% CI: [" + fdF1(c95lo) + ", " + fdUpEnd(c95hi) + "] yr",
        ("P(age " + CHAR(8805) + " 18): ") + fdPctN(p18) + "%",
        "LIMITATION: probabilistic, not a",
        "chronological determination.",
        "SDs are real spread among people.",
        "Gustafson 1950 excluded: mean error",
        "14.2 yr, Lamendin's 8.9 (39 people).",
        "Methods are NOT independent; SD is",
        "floored, not covariance-corrected."};
      rlam := 0;
      FOR widx FROM 1 TO n_est DO
        IF lbls(widx) == "Lamendin" THEN rlam := 1; END;
      END;
      IF rlam == 1 THEN
        wmsg := CONCAT(wmsg, fdLamLbl(fdLamSD()));
        wmsg := CONCAT(wmsg, {"Teaching & demonstration aid;",
                              "not for legal use."});
      END;
      fdPageV("REPORT TEMPLATE", wmsg, p18/100);
    END;
  END;

  // ================================
  // 10  THRESHOLD TABLE
  // ================================
  IF ch == 10 THEN
    IF pok == 0 THEN
      MSGBOX("No posterior yet." + NL +
             "Run Bayesian Combination first" + NL +
             "(under More options...).");
    ELSE
      // v3.6: the posterior curve.  Its Table soft key shows the
      // threshold table (the v3.5 numbers page) and Forest the forest
      // plot; each returns here, at the same threshold, until Done.
      pthr := 18;
      pact := 1;
      WHILE pact > 0 DO
        pres := fdPost(pmu, psd, pthr);
        pact := pres(1);
        pthr := pres(2);
        IF pact == 1 THEN fdThrTable(pmu, psd); END;
        IF pact == 2 THEN fdForest(means, sds, lbls, pmu, psd); END;
      END;
    END;
  END;

  // ================================
  // 11  CLEAR / NEW CASE
  // ================================
  IF ch == 11 THEN
    ch_yn := 0;                 // CANCEL must be distinguishable
    CHOOSE(ch_yn, "Clear all estimates?", "Yes", "No");
    IF ch_yn == 1 THEN
      means := {};
      sds   := {};
      lbls  := {};
      n_est := 0;
      pmu   := 0;
      psd   := 0;
      pok   := 0;
      MSGBOX("Cleared. Ready for new case." + NL +
             "Sex and ancestry settings kept.");
    END;
  END;

  // ================================
  // 12  VERIFY CALCULATIONS
  // ================================
  IF ch == 12 THEN
    // v3.9: new worked examples for the rebuilt modules (Lamendin 1992
    // with D4's 12.5, Prince-Ubelaker 2002, Bang & Ramm's own tables and
    // their several-roots formula) and a pool of P-U + Johanson + Bang &
    // Ramm.  As on the Willems and Liversidge pages, every number is
    // COMPUTED by the program's own code (fdPUAge, fdBRAge, fdBRCmb,
    // fdLamSD and the pool's arithmetic, repeated here) and printed
    // beside the answer worked by hand (verify_FDAES_v3.9a.py, Part K).
    // The scratch values use the Lamendin and Bang & Ramm modules'
    // own LOCALs; the session's pool (pmu, psd, ...) is not touched.
    lP := 3*100/15; lT := 6*100/15;
    laL := 0.18*lP + 0.42*lT + 25.53;
    laP := fdPUAge(4, 15, lP, lT);
    fdPage("ARITHMETIC SELF-CHECK", {
      "Demo: white male, one tooth, mm:",
      "RH 15, periodontosis 3, transl. 6",
      "P = 3" + mlt + "100 / 15 = " + fdF2(lP),
      "T = 6" + mlt + "100 / 15 = " + fdF2(lT),
      "LAMENDIN: 0.18" + mlt + "20 + 0.42" + mlt + "40",
      "  + 25.53 = " + fdF2(laL) + " yr",
      "  SD " + fdF1(fdLamSD()) + " (by hand 10" + mlt + (CHAR(8730) + "(" + CHAR(960) + "/2)"),
      "  = 12.53, rounded)",
      "P-U, WHITE MALES (Table 6):",
      "0.15" + mlt + "15 + 0.29" + mlt + "20 + 0.39" + mlt + "40",
      "  + 23.17 = " + fdF2(laP) + " yr, SEE 9.58",
      "By hand: 45.93 and 46.82 yr.",
      "One tooth: pool ONE of the two."});
    // Johanson (the module's own formula) and Bang & Ramm root 1
    ae := 11.02 + 5.14*1 + 2.30*1.5 + 4.14*0.5 + 3.71*0.5 + 5.57*0 + 8.98*2.5;
    brV := fdBRAge(2, 9, 6);
    fdPage("ARITHMETIC SELF-CHECK  (2)", {
      "JOHANSON (A = 1, S = 1.5, P = 0.5,",
      "C = 0.5, R = 0, T = 2.5; 2+ teeth):",
      "11.02 + 5.14 + 3.45 + 2.07 + 1.855",
      "+ 0 + 22.45 = " + STRING(ROUND(ae, 3), 2, 3) + " yr",
      "By hand: 45.985 yr.",
      "SD 5.16 yr: SD of Johanson's fit,",
      "Table 5 (46 people, 1-11 teeth each).",
      "",
      "BANG & RAMM, root 1: upper left",
      "second premolar, section, X = 6 mm",
      ("(" + CHAR(8804) + " 9.0: Table IX, row 9):"),
      "22.34 + 7.59" + mlt + "6 - 0.393" + mlt + "36",
      "= " + fdF2(brV(1)) + " yr, SA " + fdF2(brV(2)) + " (by hand 53.73)"});
    // Bang & Ramm root 2 of the same person, and the two combined
    brQ := fdBRAge(1, 13, 11);
    brCb := fdBRCmb({brV(1), brQ(1)}, {brV(2), brQ(2)});
    fdPage("ARITHMETIC SELF-CHECK  (3)", {
      "BANG & RAMM, root 2, same person:",
      "lower left lateral incisor, intact,",
      "X = 11 mm (> 9.0: Table X, row 13):",
      "33.65 + 3.53" + mlt + "11 = " + fdF2(brQ(1)) + " yr",
      "SA " + fdF2(brQ(2)) + " (B&R Fig. 16's line)",
      "ONE PERSON (p 21): A = their mean",
      "= (53.732 + 72.48) / 2 = " + fdF2(brCb(1)),
      ("S_A = (1/2) " + CHAR(8730) + "(6.88" + CHAR(178) + " + 11.12" + CHAR(178)),
      "  + 85" + mlt + "2" + mlt + "1) = " + fdF2(brCb(2)) + " yr",
      "By hand: 72.48; 63.11 and 9.23 yr.",
      ("(Without the 85: (1/2) " + CHAR(8730) + "(") + fdF2(brCb(3)) + ")",
      "= " + fdF2(brCb(3)^0.5/2) + " yr: as if independent.)",
      "Pool ONE value for this person."});
    // the pool of three, by the same arithmetic as Bayesian Combination
    // (inverse-variance weights, x 1.25, the floor at the best SD)
    brAg := {laP, ae, brCb(1)};
    brSs := {fdPUr(4), 5.16, brCb(2)};
    brOt := 0; brX := 0;
    FOR idx FROM 1 TO 3 DO
      brOt := brOt + 1/(brSs(idx)^2);
      brX := brX + brAg(idx)/(brSs(idx)^2);
    END;
    fdPage("ARITHMETIC SELF-CHECK  (4)", {
      "BAYESIAN (P-U + Joh + B&R):",
      "Prec: " + STRING(ROUND(1/brSs(1)^2, 5), 2, 5) + " + " +
        STRING(ROUND(1/brSs(2)^2, 5), 2, 5) + " + " + STRING(ROUND(1/brSs(3)^2, 5), 2, 5),
      "Total prec = " + STRING(ROUND(brOt, 5), 2, 5),
      "Mean = " + fdF2(brX/brOt) + " yr",
      "SD = SQRT(1/" + STRING(ROUND(brOt, 5), 2, 5) + ")" + mlt + "1.25",
      "   = " + STRING(ROUND((1/brOt)^0.5, 3), 2, 3) + mlt + "1.25 = " + fdF2((1/brOt)^0.5*1.25),
      "FLOORED to 5.16 (best single SD)",
      "Wts: P-U " + fdPct(100/brSs(1)^2/brOt) + "%, Joh " + fdPct(100/brSs(2)^2/brOt) +
        "%, BR " + fdPct(100/brSs(3)^2/brOt) + "%",
      "By hand: 0.01090 + 0.03756 +",
      "0.01173 = 0.06019; mean 49.47;",
      "4.076" + mlt + "1.25 = 5.09 < 5.16.",
      "Lamendin and P-U: never both (one",
      "tooth); here P-U stands for it."});
    // v3.7: the M3 rows are printed from the program's own lists
    // (fdM3Row), so this page shows exactly what the module uses.
    // v3.8: so are the Willems sums -- fdWSum on the program's own
    // tables, all seven teeth at G (stage 7) and at H (stage 8) --
    // beside the sums worked by hand from the paper's columns.
    wr := fdWSum({7, 7, 7, 7, 7, 7, 7}, 1);  wgb := wr(2);
    wr := fdWSum({7, 7, 7, 7, 7, 7, 7}, 2);  wgg := wr(2);
    wr := fdWSum({8, 8, 8, 8, 8, 8, 8}, 1);  whb := wr(2);
    wr := fdWSum({8, 8, 8, 8, 8, 8, 8}, 2);  whg := wr(2);
    fdPage("ARITHMETIC SELF-CHECK  (5)", {
      ("M3 Table 4 (erratum), P(" + CHAR(8805) + "18) %:"),
      "grades D..H; Mx maxilla, Md mandible",
      fdM3Row("Mx M", m3xm),
      fdM3Row("Mx F", m3xf),
      fdM3Row("Md M", m3dm),
      fdM3Row("Md F", m3df),
      "",
      "WILLEMS 2001, all seven teeth at G:",
      "boys:  sum of Table 1 = " + fdF2(wgb) + " yr",
      "girls: sum of Table 2 = " + fdF2(wgg) + " yr",
      "All seven at H: " + fdF2(whb) + " / " + fdF2(whg) + " yr",
      "By hand: 11.74, 11.74; 16.03 / 15.79",
      "(each a column of the paper summed)"});
    // v3.8: the Liversidge example, a boy with I2 at F, C at G and
    // P1 at E -- every figure from the program's own tables and its
    // own routine (fdLvEst), beside the answer worked by hand
    wst := {0, 6, 7, 5, 0, 0, 0};
    wlr := fdLvEst(wst, 1);
    fdPage("ARITHMETIC SELF-CHECK  (6)", {
      "LIVERSIDGE 2006 Table X, a boy:",
      "I2 F  " + fdF2(fdLvMn(1, 2, 6)) + " yr, SD " + fdF2(fdLvSD(1, 2, 6)),
      "C  G  " + fdF2(fdLvMn(1, 3, 7)) + " yr, SD " + fdF2(fdLvSD(1, 3, 7)),
      "P1 E  " + fdF2(fdLvMn(1, 4, 5)) + " yr, SD " + fdF2(fdLvSD(1, 4, 5)),
      "Mean = " + fdF2(wlr(2)*wlr(1)) + " / " + STRING(wlr(1),1,0) + " = " + fdF2(wlr(2)) + " yr",
      "SE if independent:",
      ("  " + CHAR(8730) + "(") + fdF2(fdLvSD(1, 2, 6)) + (CHAR(178) + "+") + fdF2(fdLvSD(1, 3, 7)) + (CHAR(178) + "+") +
        fdF2(fdLvSD(1, 4, 5)) + (CHAR(178) + ")/3"),
      "  = " + fdF2(wlr(3)) + " yr",
      "SE if they move together:",
      "  (" + fdF2(fdLvSD(1, 2, 6)) + "+" + fdF2(fdLvSD(1, 3, 7)) + "+" +
        fdF2(fdLvSD(1, 4, 5)) + ")/3 = " + fdF2(wlr(4)) + " yr",
      "",
      "By hand: 9.29, 0.65 and 1.11 yr.",
      "Computed from the program's tables."});
  END;

  // ================================
  // 13  ABOUT & METHODS
  // ================================
  IF ch == 13 THEN
    // v3.9 (fleet review C-F3): Lamendin et al. 1992, p 1377: "On a
    // sample of 39 individuals (42 to 79 years of age), the ME using
    // the Lamendin method was significantly lower than with the method
    // of Gustafson (8.9 +/- 2.2 versus 14.2 +/- 3.4)".  ME is a mean
    // error, not an SE; the 39 are individuals (the abstract says "39
    // teeth"; the text and Fig. 3 say individuals).
    // v3.9 (Roger, 19 Oct 2026, 12:48): About opens with what FDAES is
    // and is not
    fdPage("ABOUT FDAES v3.9d", {
      "TEACHING & DEMONSTRATION AID",
      "Not validated forensic or casework",
      "software. Do not rely on its",
      "results for legal purposes. Check",
      "every number against the original",
      "papers.",
      "",
      "Roger D. Metcalf, 2026; written with",
      "AI assistance (Anthropic Claude).",
      "Free for educational and personal",
      "use; not for sale.",
      "Instructions and assumptions: main",

      "  people it was not built from."});

      ("w_i = 1/" + CHAR(963) + "_i" + CHAR(178)),

// EXIT: the closing note as a page, then the summary line for Home

// v3.9a: the ABFO lines from the ABFO workshop "Dental Age
// Estimation: Children" (Chicago, 19-20 Feb 2011; J. M. Lewis's
// slides), slide 2: a method's data should come from a source that
// "has undergone: Review, Independent Testing, Error Estimation"

  "Estimates are probabilistic",
  "distributions, not legal age",

  // v3.8a: fdEq -- "P(age >= 18) < 0.1%" for a child, not "= < 0.1%"

  // tag "(teaching aid; not for legal use)" on the line Home keeps,
  // Roger 19 Oct 2026 and the second review; every number rounded
  // before STRING (fdF1, fdF2, fdUpEnd), so none can turn scientific)
  RETURN "FDAES (teaching aid; not for legal use): age " + fdF1(pmu) + " yr, SD " + fdF2(psd) +
         " yr, 95% CI [" + fdF1(c95lo) + ", " + fdUpEnd(c95hi) +
         ("], P(age " + CHAR(8805) + " 18)") + fdEq(fdPctN(p18)) + "%";
END;
RETURN "FDAES (teaching aid; not for legal use): no estimate";
